  ⎿  Set effort level to max (this session only): Maximum capability with deepest
      reasoning

❯ Let's do a design and brainstorming session. Based on the current DSL and .qp 
   specifications and the goals of this design, propose ideas for improvements  
  or new features. Be creative, thorough, but practical at the same time: we    
  want QProgram to evolve in a complete DSL to describe quantum programs that   
  strongly supports backwards compatibility in future versions.                 

  Searched for 2 patterns, read 2 files, listed 2 directories (ctrl+o to expand)

● I've read both specs end-to-end (DSL + .qp) and looked at the current code
  layout. Before writing, a quick framing: the spec is already in good shape on
  the vertical axis (DSL → AST → file → vendor extension), so the most useful
  brainstorm is about (a) the horizontal extensions that QProgram needs to
  actually express modern experiments, and (b) a versioning and evolution 
  framework that lets you ship those extensions without breaking files on disk.
  I'll lean heavily on the second since that's what you flagged.

  Below is the brainstorm. Each section flags pain points, gives a concrete
  proposal (often with syntax), and notes the back-compat angle.

  ---
  1. The versioning & evolution framework (lead with this)

  The current rules are sound but informal. Three independent axes need
  disciplined evolution: core spec, vendor extensions, and implicit registries
  (waveforms, schemas, block types). Today only the first two are versioned, and
   even those have under-specified edge cases.

  1.1 Make the core spec evolution rules explicit

  Pin these in the .qp spec the same way you pinned vendor compat:

  - Patch = bugfixes/clarifications only. No behavior change. Files never carry
  a patch.
  - Minor = additive only — new operations, new waveforms, new blocks, new
  optional attributes. Never changes the meaning of existing constructs.
  - Major = breaking. New parser cannot read old files without an explicit
  qp.upgrade() pass.

  Compatibility: like vendors today — same major; installed minor ≥ file minor.
  State this as a rule in §2.1, not just as guidance.

  1.2 Feature flags as a finer-grained dial

  Minor-version-as-monotonic-set is too coarse once you have multiple parallel
  feature tracks (e.g., mid-circuit feedback, classification, persistent
  results, gate library). Adopt a feature-flags layer on top of versions, in the
   spirit of SQL standards and Rust editions:

  #!QProgram 1.3
  features: classification mid_circuit_feedback named_measurements

  - A parser accepts a file if file.major == parser.major, file.minor ≤ 
  parser.minor, and file.features ⊆ parser.features.
  - Features are documented as named bundles in the spec, each with its own
  grammar fragment. New experimental features land as opt-in feature flags
  before becoming default-on in a minor bump.
  - This means experimental constructs can ship in 1.3 without poisoning every
  1.3 file — you only pay if you use them.

  1.3 Make require style consistent across all extension axes

  Today vendors get require. Custom user-registered waveforms get nothing — a
  .qp containing MyCustomPulse(...) just hopes the loader has the class. Lift
  the require mechanism to a generic dependency block:

  require vendor qblox 0.1
  require waveforms qililab_pulses 1.2
  require schema transmon_extended 1.0

  Each require line carries a category, a name, and a version. The runtime
  registry maps (category, name) → version + class table. Same compat rules as
  vendors. This is what lets .qp files be true contracts.

  1.4 Always emit inline schemas (or version the presets)

  Right now schema: transmon is brittle: if BusSchema.transmon() ever grows a
  bus, every old .qp silently inherits the new bus on load. Two ways out:

  - (Recommended) Always serialize the expanded inline form. The preset is
  purely a Python construction-time convenience; the file records the canonical
  structure. Round-trip: BusSchema.transmon() → inline-on-disk → dynamic schema
  with the right elements on load. A tiny # schema: transmon (expanded) header
  comment preserves the human breadcrumb.
  - Or, version the presets: schema: transmon@1.0. Adds another moving part for
  no real benefit.

  1.5 Deprecation contract

  Add a formal deprecation lane to both the Python API and the file format:

  - Python: qp.deprecated(since="1.2", removed_in="2.0", 
  replacement="new_thing") decorator emitting DeprecationWarning.
  - .qp: a deprecated operation keeps parsing in the same major; serializer
  rewrites it to the replacement on save (with an option to preserve the
  original token for grep-stability).
  - Spec section "Deprecated constructs" lists every name that was once valid
  and what it became.

  1.6 Migration tool

  qp upgrade path.qp --target 1.5 and qp upgrade path.qp --to 2.0. Mechanical
  rewriter that walks the AST and applies a registered set of rewrites. Same
  tool used for vendor extension upgrades (qp upgrade path.qp --vendor 
  qblox=0.2). This is the thing that makes a major bump feel safe rather than
  scary.

  1.7 Round-trip golden tests in CI

  Mention in the spec: implementations MUST ship a corpus of .qp files that
  exercise every grammar rule and feature flag, with loads(dumps(loads(file))) 
  == loads(file) checks. Without this discipline backwards compat will erode
  silently.

  ---
  2. Expression system: it stops at arithmetic, but it shouldn't

  Section 3 has + - * / and unary -/+. That's enough for sweep math but blocks
  every interesting feature downstream — conditionals, classification,
  parametric waveforms, units checks.

  2.1 Comparisons and booleans

  pred = (freq < 5e9) & (gain >= 0.3)

  Add ==, !=, <, <=, >, >= and logical &, |, ~ (or named and_, or_, not_ to
  avoid Python truthiness traps). Result type is a new BoolExpression. .qp
  syntax: same operators, with && / || / ! in the grammar to avoid the
  bitwise/logical confusion.

  2.2 Math functions as first-class expressions

  sin, cos, tan, exp, log, sqrt, abs, min, max, clip. They're typed and
  evaluatable like any other Expression. Crucial for waveform parameterization
  (amplitude = base * cos(phase)) and host-side derived sweeps.

  2.3 Conditional expression

  amp = where(state == 1, recovery_amp, 0.0)

  Two-arm select. Required for any classification-driven feedback in
  expressions, e.g., feeding a reset amplitude.

  2.4 Integer vs float distinction (optional)

  Today Variable carries units (free-form string) but no dtype. Hardware
  sequencers care — frequencies and durations often live in different register
  types. Proposal:

  - Add dtype: Literal["int", "float", "bool"] to Variable, optional, default
  float.
  - Operations that need integers (e.g., wait) require an int-typed variable;
  the compiler can warn at construction time.
  - Keep this non-load-bearing: it's metadata for compilers/typecheckers, not a
  runtime gate. Don't repeat the Domain enum mistake — let it be a string with a
   known small set.

  2.5 Standard unit vocabulary

  units is free-form today. Suggest a documented set (Hz, s, ns, V, A, rad, dB,
  dimensionless ""). Compiler can warn on type-incoherent ops (set_frequency
  expects Hz). Still free-form — but the registered set gets validation.

  2.6 Backwards-compat angle

  All of §2 lands behind feature flags (comparisons, math_funcs, dtype_metadata)
   in features:. Files using only + - * / keep parsing on every existing parser.

  ---
  3. Control flow: the conditional gap is the biggest

  The DSL today has parallel, average, for, loop, block — but no if. That's why
  active_reset had to become a vendor primitive, and why every
  measurement-conditioned protocol (post-selection, real-time error correction,
  leakage detection) is currently a vendor-monolith op. This is the single
  largest expressiveness gap.

  3.1 if / else_if / else blocks

  state = program.classify(measurement, threshold=0.0)

  with program.if_(state == 1):
      program.play(q[0].drive, "pi_pulse")
  with program.else_():
      program.wait(q[0].drive, 40)

  .qp:
  if state == 1:
    play q[0].drive "pi_pulse"
  else:
    wait q[0].drive 40

  The compiler is free to lower this to a hardware branch (FPGA conditional
  pulse on qblox, real-time Boolean on QM), or to a software branch if it's an
  outer-loop condition. Same hardware-vs-software principle as loops.

  3.2 repeat(n) (non-averaging counterpart of average)

  average(N) collapses the dimension. repeat(N) keeps each shot. RB and
  tomography need it. Today you'd sweep a dummy variable. Make it explicit.

  3.3 Bounded while

  with program.while_(predicate, max_iter=100):
      ...

  Always with a max_iter bound — unbounded while is a footgun on hardware.
  Useful for adaptive protocols.

  3.4 break / continue

  Useful inside for_loop for "stop on first failure" or "retry-on-bad-shot"
  patterns. Easy to compile to software, harder to hardware — but the language
  should not preclude it.

  3.5 Explicit parallel block (not just on loops)

  Today | parallelizes loops. But sometimes you want two pulse sequences to
  start concurrently on different buses without any sweep:

  with program.parallel():
      with program.block():
          program.play(q[0].drive, pi)
      with program.block():
          program.play(q[1].drive, pi_2)

  Same data model as loop-parallel — a Parallel block whose children execute
  concurrently. Composes with existing sync().

  3.6 align() / align_left() / align_right() / barrier()

  sync aligns ends. Often you want left-alignment (start together),
  right-alignment (end together), or a logical barrier with no concrete sync.
  Borrow vocab from OpenQASM 3: barrier is the broad term; align_* is the timing
   semantic.

  3.7 Back-compat

  All conditional / branching constructs land behind a feature: 
  mid_circuit_feedback flag. repeat lands behind feature: repeat_block. align is
   additive — a new minor.

  ---
  4. Measurement, classification, and the results pipeline

  measure() returns I/Q and average(N) collapses it. The pipeline upstream of
  result.get(measurement=0) is rich; the language only exposes a thin slice.

  4.1 Named measurements

  measurement=0 indexing is fragile — insert a measurement and every downstream
  index shifts.

  program.measure(q[0].readout, "readout", "weights", name="rabi_pt")
  result.get("rabi_pt")

  .qp: measure q[0].readout "readout" "weights" name="rabi_pt". Optional;
  defaults to m0, m1, ... Backwards-compat: name-resolution falls back to
  positional when name= is absent.

  4.2 classify as a core operation

  state = program.classify(measurement, threshold=0.0)            # 2-state, 
  simple threshold
  state = program.classify(measurement, kind="kmeans", states=3)  # 3-state, 
  calibrated

  Returns a Variable that can flow into if_. Concrete classifier definition is
  platform-side; the DSL only knows "this measurement gets reduced to an integer
   state". This is the bridge that makes §3.1 actually useful.

  4.3 single_shot(shots=N) / repeat(N) keeping shot dimension

  Today average(N) collapses. Many experiments need the per-shot histogram for
  cross-correlations or post-selection. Add single_shot(shots=N) block; results
  gain a shot dimension.

  4.4 Measurement projections

  measure(..., return=["I", "Q"]) / return=["state"] / return=["I", "Q", 
  "state"]. Lets the compiler decide what to ship back. Default unchanged (I, Q)
   for compat.

  4.5 save_adc becomes part of return

  measure(..., return=["raw"]) is cleaner than a separate boolean. Keep
  save_adc=True as alias-mapped-to-return=["I","Q","raw"] for one version with a
   deprecation warning.

  4.6 Post-selection markers

  program.tag(measurement, predicate) annotates which shots to keep. Useful at
  analysis time; doesn't change the hardware program. Lives in the result
  coords.

  ---
  5. Waveforms: shape catalog + algebra

  5.1 Waveform algebra

  A Waveform is a function of time. Treat it as such — let users compose:

  wf = Gaussian(0.5, 40, 2.5) + Square(0.1, 40)   # pointwise sum
  wf = 0.5 * IQDrag(...)                          # amplitude scale
  wf = Square(1.0, 100) @ -20                     # time-shifted by -20ns 
  (pad-left)
  wf = wf1.chained_with(wf2)                      # explicit instead of 
  Chained([...])

  Backed by lazy AST nodes (WaveformSum, WaveformScale, WaveformShift) so they
  serialize round-trip. Saves users from building one-off subclasses for every
  composite shape.

  5.2 Broader built-in catalog

  Practical additions: Cosine, Tukey, Blackman, GaussianFlatTop, HermiteGate,
  Modulated(envelope, frequency, phase), Chirp. Each is a minor-bump feature
  flag (feature: waveforms_v2).

  5.3 Waveform libraries

  Currently with_waveforms({...}) is per-program. Add WaveformLibrary as a
  first-class object that can be loaded from disk and shared across programs:

  lib = qp.WaveformLibrary.from_qp("calibrations/2026-05-13.qpl")
  resolved = program.with_waveforms(lib)

  .qpl (or whatever extension) reuses the existing waveform serializer — it's
  just a .qp file containing only waveform definitions. Calibration tooling
  writes these on every fit; programs reference them by path or hash.

  5.4 Sample-rate awareness

  Some buses sample at 1 GS/s, others at 2 GS/s. Today get_duration() -> int 
  (ns) is rate-agnostic but envelope(resolution=1) is rate-aware. Make this
  explicit: envelope(sample_rate_hz=1e9). Doesn't break old code (the kwarg is
  optional).

  ---
  6. Bus schema: capabilities and connectivity

  6.1 Connectivity graph

  A schema can declare which qubits are coupled:

  schema = BusSchema.transmon_coupled()
  schema.couple(q[0], q[1], via=c[0,1])
  schema.couple(q[1], q[2], via=c[1,2])

  Compilers can validate two-qubit gates against connectivity. .qp:
  schema:
    ...
    edges:
      q[0]-q[1] via c[0,1]
      q[1]-q[2] via c[1,2]

  6.2 Per-bus capability annotations

  Already in spec: channel, acquires. Add optional frequency_range,
  max_amplitude, min_pulse_duration. These are platform-coupled, but if a schema
   is platform-provided, attaching them makes static validation possible:

  schema:
    element q:
      drive   info=IQ   freq_range=4e9..8e9 max_amp=1.0
      readout info=IQ+acquires

  Optional and feature-flagged; old parsers ignore unknown info tokens.

  6.3 Element aliases

  Sometimes q reads worse than qubits. Allow:

  schema = BusSchema.transmon().alias("q", "qubit", "qubits")

  So schema.qubit[0].drive == schema.q[0].drive. Cosmetic, but big DX win in
  notebooks.

  ---
  7. Composability: subprograms, gates, calibration

  7.1 Subprograms / fragments

  Right now reuse happens via Python functions building parts of a program.
  That's fine for Python users but invisible to .qp. Add named fragments:

  @qp.fragment
  def pi_pulse(program, bus):
      program.play(bus, "pi_pulse")
      program.wait(bus, 4)

  In .qp:
  fragment pi_pulse(bus):
    play bus "pi_pulse"
    wait bus 4

  body:
    pi_pulse(q[0].drive)

  The fragment is a parameterized block. Inlines at compile time (or stays as a
  call node if the compiler prefers). Lets .qp files express RB sequences
  without explosion.

  7.2 A canonical gate layer (optional, vendor-extension shape)

  Don't bake gates into core. Instead, ship a qprogram-gates reference extension
   that registers gate.x(q), gate.cz(q1,q2), etc., each lowering to pulses via a
   calibration table. Demonstrates: core stays pulse-level, the gate language is
   a vendor extension like any other. Backwards compat is the same require gate 
  0.1 story.

  7.3 Calibration as a first-class object

  Calibration carries qubit frequencies, T1/T2, gate parameters. Attach to a
  program:

  program.with_calibration(cal).with_waveforms(cal.waveforms)

  .qp references calibration by path/hash (not embedded — calibration is huge).
  Loader fails if hash mismatches.

  ---
  8. Vendor extensions: a few sharper edges

  The three-layer architecture is good. What's missing:

  8.1 Vendor block types (not just operations)

  Vendors should be able to register control-flow blocks, not only operations.
  E.g., qblox might want a with program.qblox.trigger_scope(): that's
  transparent at the language level but means something to the compiler. Add a
  registry mirror of operations:

  register_vendor_block("qblox", "trigger_scope", QbloxTriggerScope)

  .qp syntax: qblox.trigger_scope: followed by indented body.

  8.2 Vendor-specific waveforms

  Some vendors have native pulse shapes. Today there's only one global waveform
  registry. Add register_vendor_waveform("qblox", "PsbPulse", PsbPulse).
  Serializes as qblox.PsbPulse(...).

  8.3 Capabilities discovery API

  QProgram.vendor_capabilities("qblox")
  # -> {"operations": [...], "blocks": [...], "waveforms": [...], "version": 
  "0.1.0"}

  Lets tooling inspect what a vendor exposes without scraping the registry.

  8.4 Conflict policy across vendors

  Two vendors define acquire — what happens? Today: dotted namespace prevents
  collisions (qblox.acquire vs qm.acquire). Keep that, but state it explicitly:
  vendor ops can never collide with core ops or with each other at the language
  level.

  8.5 Vendor upgrade hooks

  Vendor extensions ship a register_vendor_upgrader(from_version, to_version, 
  fn) so the qp upgrade tool knows how to rewrite operations across vendor
  versions. E.g., qblox 0.2 might rename set_markers to set_marker_mask and
  set_markers rewrites mechanically.

  ---
  9. Tooling and DX (the boring multipliers)

  These don't move the spec, but they move adoption:

  - Formatter — qp fmt. Pin a canonical layout so diffs stay clean.
  - Linter — qp lint: unused variables, missing sync between non-sync'd buses,
  very long sweeps without average, schema-incompatible operations, undefined
  waveform aliases. Configurable severity.
  - Dry-run / analyzer — qp analyze program.qp reports estimated wall time,
  total shots, measurement count, max nesting depth, distinct buses used,
  undeclared aliases.
  - Diagram — qp draw program.qp -o timeline.svg renders pulse timeline. Big
  notebook win.
  - Diff — qp diff a.qp b.qp semantic diff (ignore reordering of commutative
  operations).
  - LSP — language server for .qp files: autocomplete on bus paths once the
  schema is resolved, jump to fragment definitions, hover docs.

  ---
  10. Platform protocol

  The PlatformProtocol is minimal today. Three small additions pay off:

  10.1 compile(program) -> CompiledProgram

  Separate compile from execute so callers can cache, profile, or transmit
  compiled programs. execute becomes a thin wrapper.

  10.2 estimate(program) -> Estimate

  Pre-flight check: estimated duration, resource usage, list of warnings. Cheap;
   lets notebooks gate long runs.

  10.3 A common error taxonomy

  UnsupportedOperationError, BusNotAvailableError, WaveformResolutionError,
  CompilationError, HardwareError — a small set of base exceptions that all
  platforms must use. Section 12 of the DSL spec ("Error model") was an open
  question; this is its answer.

  ---
  11. Things I'd flag as genuine design tensions, not free wins

  Worth a separate discussion before committing:

  1. schema: preset round-trip — keep the keyword or always expand? I lean
  expand, but the keyword's nicer for humans. Maybe both — keyword form for
  input, expanded form for output, with a comment header.
  2. Conditionals on hardware — adding if_ is easy at the language level but
  hard at the compiler level. The DSL should not commit to when a backend can
  execute conditionals in real time; that's a per-platform capability flag.
  3. Where does the gate language live? A reference vendor extension (my
  preference, keeps core clean) vs a first-class core layer (more visible to
  users, but bloats core). Pick one and stick with it.
  4. Free-form units/dtype vs an enum — enums are safer but force every new unit
   into a release. Free-form is flexible but doesn't actually validate anything.
   The hybrid (free-form with a known canonical set that gets validation) is
  what I suggested in §2.5; it's a slight inconsistency but practical.
  5. name= on measurements is great but doubles the indexing API. Could go
  further: deprecate positional indexing entirely in a major bump. That's a
  sharper choice; might be worth it.
  6. Feature flags vs minor versions — adopting both is genuinely more
  complexity. Worth it iff the project expects to land >3 substantive
  experimental features in the next year. Otherwise minor-bumps-only is simpler.

  ---
  TL;DR recommended ordering

  If you wanted a sequenced roadmap, I'd land it roughly:

  - v1.1 (no-risk additive): named measurements, repeat block, broader waveform
  catalog, align/barrier, qp fmt + golden round-trip tests.
  - v1.2 (the expression upgrade): comparisons, booleans, math funcs, optional
  dtype on variables, conditional expression where(). Behind feature flags.
  - v1.3 (the feedback story): classify, if_/else_, single_shot, named
  measurements feeding into if_. Behind mid_circuit_feedback flag.
  - v1.4 (composability): fragments, calibration object, waveform libraries.
  - v1.5 (tooling polish): linter, analyzer, diagram, LSP.
  - v2.0 (only if needed): drop deprecated positional indexing; rework anything
  that the feature-flag escape hatch couldn't paper over.

  Throughout, every release ships its qp upgrade rewrites, its golden corpus,
  and its deprecation entries — so v1.0 files keep loading on every parser
  through v2.0 with at most a one-shot mechanical upgrade.

  Which of these areas do you want to drill into first? I'd guess the
  conditional/feedback story or the versioning framework, but I'll follow your
  lead.

✻ Baked for 3m 41s

※ recap: We're brainstorming improvements to the QProgram DSL and .qp spec with a focus on backwards compatibility. I delivered a structured proposal covering versioning, expressions, control flow, measurement, waveforms, and tooling. Next: pick an area to drill into. (disable recaps in /config)

❯ First of all, we are still on the first implementation / release phase, so    
  any change we do NOW don't need to be backwards compatible, but we should     
  design with backwards compatibility in mind for future releases. We should    
  design so future changes are as few as, as less intrusive as possible.        

● Good reframing. The right lens then isn't "how do we evolve safely?" but "what
   shapes are most expensive to retrofit later?" Everything that would force a
  grammar change, a parser branch, or a breaking API change for an additive
  feature is what we want to design out now. Below is reorganized around exactly
   that — extension points to bake in before 1.0, and a few things in the
  current spec that would be cheaper to redesign than to live with.

  ---
  1. Registry-driven everything (highest leverage)

  This is the single architectural decision with the most downstream
  consequences. The vendor-ops path already does this well via inspect.signature
   + (vendor, name) → cls registry. Apply the same pattern everywhere a new 
  "kind of thing" could ever be added. That means:

  Surface: Core operations
  Today: One parser branch per op in _parse_operation
  Make it: Single generic parser keyed off an operation registry (mirror of
    vendor's)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Surface: Built-in waveforms
  Today: Registry-driven ✓
  Make it: Keep as-is
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Surface: Block types
  Today: Hardcoded for/loop/average/parallel/block keywords + custom AST nodes
  Make it: Block-type registry; vendors can register blocks too
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Surface: Schema presets
  Today: Hardcoded keyword list in grammar (transmon, fluxonium, ...)
  Make it: Always serialize expanded inline — drop the preset keyword from the
    file format entirely
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Surface: Sweep generators
  Today: range(...) and [...] and file(...) in grammar
  Make it: Sweep generator registry — range, linspace, logspace, values, file,
    future random, meshgrid
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Surface: Metadata fields
  Today: label, description only
  Make it: Free-form key: value bag in metadata:
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Surface: Operation attributes
  Today: Positional + a few named
  Make it: Trailing key=value* attribute bag

  The principle: anything the spec currently enumerates by name should be a 
  registry, not a grammar production. Adding a new preset, sweep type, or block
  type then ships as a Python registration, not a parser change. The grammar
  reduces to a small set of generic shapes that all extensions slot into.

  Concretely, the .qp grammar collapses to roughly:

  operation     := (VENDOR ".")? OP_NAME positional* attribute*
  attribute     := IDENT "=" value
  control_block := BLOCK_NAME positional* attribute* ":" body
  sweep_block   := "for" IDENT "in" sweep_expr attribute* ":" body
  sweep_expr    := SWEEP_NAME "(" arg_list ")"

  That's the whole language. Everything else lives in registries.

  ---
  2. The attribute-bag pattern (the single most useful syntactic move)

  Today most things in .qp are positional with a fixed shape: play "bus" pulse,
  measure "bus" wf weights, var freq label="..." units="...". The var line
  already shows the right pattern — anything growable becomes a trailing
  key="value" bag.

  Make this universal, starting now:

  play   q[0].drive   "pi"                                       # 1.0
  play   q[0].drive   "pi"   amplitude_scale=0.5                 # 1.x — same
  parser
  measure q[0].readout "r" "w" name="rabi_pt" return="iq,state"  # 1.x — same
  parser
  average shots=1000 reset_between=true:                         # 1.x — same
  parser
  for freq in range(4e9, 6e9, 1e6) chunk=100:                     # 1.x — same
  parser

  The benefit isn't readability (it's about the same) — it's that a parser
  written today reads every future addition without ever changing the grammar.
  Old parsers can be configured to either skip-with-warning or hard-error on
  unknown attributes; new attributes never collide with old ones.

  This is not the same as named arguments in Python. Python keeps both
  positional and keyword for ergonomics. The point is: in the serialized form,
  anything optional / extensible lives in the attribute bag.

  ---
  3. Widen the Expression AST to a single typed tree — now, before consumers 
  calcify

  Today Expression is numeric-only: + - * /, unary. Every consumer (writer,
  parser, validators, evaluator, variables() walker) assumes this. The moment
  you add BoolExpression for comparisons, every consumer needs widening — and
  there will be more consumers by then (linter, analyzer, LSP, diagram tool).

  Land the wider hierarchy now, even if 1.0 doesn't expose if_:

  Expression
  ├─ Leaves
  │  ├─ Variable             (id, dtype, units, label, description)
  │  ├─ Constant             (value, dtype)
  │  └─ MeasurementHandle    (see §4)
  ├─ Arithmetic              (+ - * /, unary)
  ├─ Comparison              (== != < <= > >=)         ← reserved syntax,
  exposed in 1.0
  ├─ Logical                 (and or not)              ← reserved syntax
  ├─ Math                    (sin cos exp log sqrt abs min max)
  └─ Conditional             (where(cond, then, else))

  You don't need to enable if_(predicate) in 1.0. You just need the AST node
  types to exist, the serializer to know how to round-trip them, and evaluate()
  to handle them. Then 1.x adds if_ as a pure block type with zero grammar
  churn.

  Also: add dtype to Variable now ("int" | "float" | "bool", default "float").
  Don't enforce it — but having the field present means compilers can read it
  without an AST change later. Free metadata is the cheapest hedge there is.

  ---
  4. measure() returns a handle — the keystone for future feedback

  This is small but critical. Today measure() returns None. The moment
  classification + if_ arrives, every existing measurement call site has to be
  rewritten to capture a return value. Bite that bullet today:

  m0 = program.measure(q[0].readout, "readout", "weights")   # returns 
  MeasurementHandle
  program.measure(q[0].readout, "readout", "weights")        # discarding handle
   is fine

  The MeasurementHandle:

  - carries the auto-assigned name ("m0") and can be renamed via name= kwarg;
  - is the identifier used to retrieve results (result.get(m0) works, alongside
  result.get("m0"));
  - is the argument that future classify(handle, ...) and predicate-builders
  take;
  - serializes in .qp as the name only — measure q[0].readout "r" "w" name="m0".

  Same idea applies to get_parameter, which already returns a Variable.
  Establish the convention everywhere that "an operation that produces a
  referenceable thing returns a handle to it" before patterns set in.

  ---
  5. Unify sweep blocks behind a single block + pluggable sweep generators

  Currently for_loop(var, start, stop, step) and loop(var, values) are two
  different block classes. The | parallel works on both. But every future sweep
  type (linspace, logspace, random, conditional dependent sweeps) would need
  either a new block class or some shoehorning into one of the existing two.

  Collapse to one:

  program.sweep(var, Range(start, stop, step))          # was for_loop
  program.sweep(var, Values(np.array([...])))           # was loop
  program.sweep(var, Linspace(start, stop, n))          # new — free addition
  program.sweep(var, Logspace(start, stop, n))          # new — free addition
  program.sweep(var, FromFile("path"))                  # new — free addition

  Internally one Sweep block class; the sweep generator is the registered,
  extensible piece. for_loop and loop can stick around as thin convenience
  wrappers if you want familiar names — but the AST has one node type and .qp
  has one syntax. Then | and parallel composition work uniformly without
  per-loop-type special-casing.

  .qp syntax remains for var in <generator>(...); the generator name is just a
  registry lookup.

  ---
  6. Operation introspection contract

  The Operation ABC today has get_variables(). Lock down the full contract now,
  so every later analyzer/compiler/linter has uniform hooks:

  class Operation(ABC):
      name: ClassVar[str]                                # registered op name
      def variables(self) -> set[Variable]: ...          # free variables 
  referenced
      def buses(self) -> set[str]: ...                   # bus refs referenced
      def waveforms(self) -> set[Waveform | str]: ...    # waveforms/aliases 
  referenced
      def attributes(self) -> dict[str, Any]: ...        # serialize attribute 
  bag
      def visit(self, visitor): ...                      # standard visitor hook

  Same for Block. The minute someone writes qp lint or qp analyze or
  platform.estimate() they're going to want exactly this set of hooks. Defining
  them now means new operations from vendors plug in for free.

  visit() matters: if every node has a uniform visitor hook from day one, the
  writer, parser, validators, and any future tool are decoupled from concrete
  node types. Without it you get a thicket of isinstance chains.

  ---
  7. Names everywhere instead of positional indexing

  Anywhere the user might refer to the n-th thing — measurements, blocks for
  debugging, traces — give it a stable name from construction. Positional
  indexing is a back-compat hazard the moment anyone reorders code or inserts
  something:

  - Measurements: auto-named m0, m1, ...; name= overrides. result.get("m0") is
  the primary API; result.get(0) is just sugar.
  - Blocks (for tooling, not user code): auto IDs assigned at construction so
  the AST is addressable by stable path. Not exposed in .qp (it'd be noise), but
   lives in memory.
  - Variables: already named by id. ✓

  The cheapest possible change with the largest reduction in future API churn.

  ---
  8. Reserve keywords and dotted-namespace prefixes now

  Avoid the JavaScript "we should have reserved let" problem. Reserve in the
  lexer:

  if, else, elif, while, until, break, continue, return, fragment, def, gate,
  case, match, repeat, barrier, align, align_left, align_right, parallel, where,
   let, const, import, from, as, true, false, null.

  Same in Python: these names can't be used as variable ids, bus element names,
  operation names, or vendor names. Cheaper to reserve and not use than to claw
  back later.

  Also pick a convention now: dotted prefixes are reserved for vendor namespaces
   only. core.something should be disallowed (or reserved). Period in operation
  names is the vendor sigil and nothing else.

  ---
  9. Drop the preset schema keyword from the file format

  Keep BusSchema.transmon() etc. in the Python API — they're great construction
  helpers. But in .qp, always serialize as the expanded inline form. No schema: 
  transmon keyword in the grammar at all.

  Why now: the second a transmon() preset grows a new bus, every existing
  schema: transmon file silently inherits it. Inline form pins the structure to
  whatever it was when the file was written. The cost is a handful of lines of
  extra serialization output, paid once. The benefit is the schema axis stops
  being a versioning surface forever.

  Same answer as "always emit inline" in my previous response — but with the v1
  reframing, this is now cheap to just do, not a deprecation cycle.

  ---
  10. Versioning skeleton, even at 0.x

  Don't punt the version handling until 1.0. Implement now:

  - Writer always emits #!QProgram <major>.<minor>. Pre-1.0 it's #!QProgram 0.1.
   Fine.
  - Parser validates the header strictly: same major, parser minor ≥ file minor.
  - Vendor require already does this ✓.
  - qprogram.SPEC_VERSION constant; qprogram.spec_version() accessor.

  Pre-1.0 you can bump majors freely (0.x → 0.y is "anything goes"). The point
  is the machinery exists, so 1.0 doesn't have to retrofit it.

  ---
  11. Hashability and structural equality on AST nodes

  Make Waveform, Operation, Block, Expression (sans Variable, which is
  identity-based on purpose) all hashable and structurally equal. Implement
  once, get for free:

  - AST diffing for tools
  - Dedup of identical waveforms in serialized output
  - Cache keys for compiled programs
  - Programmatic graph rewrites (program.transform(pattern, replacement))

  This is a one-time discipline (make every constructor parameter immutable,
  implement __hash__/__eq__) that closes off the entire "we need to compare
  programs" can of worms.

  ---
  12. Decouple results from execution by defining the result API now

  QProgramResult is already specced as in-memory + xarray. The thing to lock
  down before 1.0 is the API surface, not the storage:

  class QProgramResult:
      def get(self, *, measurement: str | int | MeasurementHandle = 0, bus: str 
  | None = None) -> xr.DataArray: ...
      def measurements(self) -> dict[str, xr.DataArray]: ...   # all of them
      def to_dataset(self) -> xr.Dataset: ...                  # everything in 
  one xr.Dataset
      def metadata(self) -> dict[str, Any]: ...                # run-time 
  metadata

  The to_dataset() method matters: it's the answer to "results from many
  measurements with shared sweep coordinates" without inventing a custom
  container. xarray's Dataset already nails it.

  ---
  13. Things in the current spec to redesign before 1.0 freezes them

  Quick punch list of cheap-now / expensive-later fixes:

  1. save_adc: bool on measure → replace with return: list[str] or
  returns="iq,state,raw" attribute. The boolean has no room to grow; the
  attribute is the obvious place for classification, raw traces, alternative
  projections.
  2. set_offset(bus, offset_path0, offset_path1=None) — the path0/path1 naming
  leaks Qblox vocabulary into core. Generalize to set_offset(bus, *values) or
  set_offset(bus, i=..., q=...), and let vendors specialize via attributes.
  3. set_parameter channel_id — already a kwarg, fine, but consider making it
  part of the attribute bag rather than a fixed parameter.
  4. average(shots) is a meaning (collapse-to-mean) bundled with a count. Split:
   a repeat(N) block exists and an average=true attribute on it (or on measure)
  means "collapse this dimension". More composable, lets you sweep over per-shot
   data without inventing a parallel "single_shot" block later.
  5. CrosstalkMatrix in §7 — flagged with (?) in the spec already. Either commit
   to it as a first-class object or move it to a vendor extension. Don't ship
  1.0 with a maybe.
  6. parallel() legacy mention — already removed in favor of |. ✓ keep it
  removed.
  7. Open question on error model (§12) — define the base exception taxonomy now
   even if implementations are minimal. QProgramError → 
  UnsupportedOperationError | BusNotAvailableError | WaveformResolutionError | 
  CompilationError | HardwareError. Adding new subclasses later is non-breaking;
   changing the base hierarchy later is.

  ---
  14. Two architectural decisions worth deciding explicitly

  These have downstream consequences and shouldn't be left implicit:

  (A) Are vendor extensions allowed to register block types and waveforms, not 
  only operations?
  Implication if yes (recommended): the vendor registry has three sub-registries
   (operations, blocks, waveforms), and parsers/writers look up by (vendor, 
  kind, name). Implication if no: vendors are forever limited to leaf
  operations, and complex orchestration is always a single fat op.

  (B) Does an Operation know its own "kind" (real-time pulse / parameter set / 
  software-orchestration)?
  Recommendation: no. Keep the spec's stated design — the compiler classifies.
  But document the contract: a vendor op declares only its data and references;
  the platform decides scheduling. This is already implicit; making it explicit
  prevents future temptation to leak a kind enum into core.

  ---
  15. The lock-it-down-before-1.0 list, ordered

  If you want one list of "make these structural changes before stamping 1.0":

  1. Generic registry-driven parser & writer for all op kinds, blocks, sweep
  generators (§1).
  2. Attribute-bag syntax in the file format, replacing growth-prone positional
  args (§2).
  3. Widen Expression AST to include comparison/logical/conditional/math node
  types (§3).
  4. measure() returns a MeasurementHandle (§4).
  5. Collapse for_loop/loop into one Sweep block + sweep-generator registry
  (§5).
  6. Lock down Operation / Block introspection contract (variables, buses,
  waveforms, attributes, visit) (§6).
  7. Names everywhere for indexable artifacts (§7).
  8. Reserve future keywords in lexer + Python API (§8).
  9. Always inline-serialize schemas; drop the preset keyword (§9).
  10. Wire up version validation now (§10).
  11. Make AST nodes hashable + structurally equal (§11).
  12. Lock the QProgramResult API surface (§12).
  13. Clean up the seven items in §13 before they freeze.

  Each of these is cheap today and would be a deprecation cycle tomorrow. After
  they're in, almost every plausible future feature — conditionals,
  classification, gate libraries, vendor blocks, new sweep types, mid-circuit
  feedback — slots in as a registration or a new AST node, not a grammar change.

  Want me to start drafting any of these as concrete spec changes? The two with
  the biggest blast radius — and worth doing first — are #1 (registry-driven
  everything) and #3 (widened Expression AST).

  the spec callable types to use Any since the dispatch is intentionally
  type-erased.

● Update(qprogram/src/qprogram/serialization/registry.py)

      44 +# Callback signatures are intentionally type-erased: the regist
         +ry stores                                                      
      45 +# callbacks for concrete subclasses (e.g. ``sync_serialize`` ta
         +kes ``Sync``,                                                  
      46 +# not ``Operation``) and the writer/parser is the only call sit
         +e. Typing                                                      
      47 +# them as ``Callable[[Operation, Any], str]`` would force every
         + registration                                                  
      48 +# to cast or accept a wider base; ``Any`` lets the natural per-
         +class signature                                                
      49 +# survive and keeps callbacks readable.

      51 +OperationParseFn = Callable[[list[str], Any], Any]

       1 +"""Parser for the ``.qp`` file format.                         
       2 +                                                               
       3 +Like the writer, the parser is intentionally thin. Header/requi
         +re/metadata                                                    
       4 +parsing is fixed grammar, but everything inside ``body:`` — ope
         +rations,                                                       
       5 +control-flow blocks, sweep generators — is dispatched through t
         +he registries                                                  
       6 +in :mod:`qprogram.serialization.registry`. New operations, bloc
         +ks, or sweep                                                   
       7 +sources can be added by registration alone; no parser change re
         +quired.                                                        
       8 +                                                               
       9 +The parser exposes a small *parse context* API used by spec cal
         +lbacks:                                                        
      10 +                                                               
      11 +- :meth:`parse_value` — token → typed value (string / number / 
         +bool / list /                                                  
      12 +  inline waveform / declared variable).                        
      13 +- :meth:`parse_error` — produce a :class:`ParseError` tagged wi
         +th the current                                                 
      14 +  line number, for use by callbacks that detect bad input.

         +et_parameter``)

      430 +    def _try_parse_block_header(self, parent: Block, line: str
          +, min_indent: int) -> bool:                                   
      431 +        """Return True iff ``line`` was a block header (and th
          +e block was parsed)."""                                       
      432 +        if not line.endswith(":"):                            
      433 +            return False                                      
      434 +        header = line[:-1].rstrip()                           
      435 +        # Loop family: ``for`` heads either a single loop or a
          + parallel of loops.                                           
      436 +        if header.startswith("for ") or ("|" in header and "fo
          +r " in header):                                               
      437 +            self._parse_loop_or_parallel(parent, header, min_i
          +ndent)                                                        
      438 +            return True                                       
      439 +        # Keyword-led block: leading word maps to a registered
          + BlockSpec.                                                   
      440 +        first_token, _, rest = header.partition(" ")          
      441 +        spec = get_block_spec(first_token)                    
      442 +        if spec is None:                                      
      443 +            return False                                      
      444 +        tokens = _tokenize(rest) if rest.strip() else []      
      445 +        if spec.parse_header is not None:                     
      446 +            block = spec.parse_header(tokens, self)           
      447 +        else:                                                 
      448 +            block = spec.cls()                                
      449 +        parent.append(block)                                  
      450 +        self._pos += 1                                        
      451 +        self._parse_statements(block, min_indent + 2)         
      452 +        return True                                           
      453 +                                                              
      454 +    def _parse_loop_or_parallel(self, parent: Block, header: s
          +tr, min_indent: int) -> None:                                 
      455 +        loop_parts = [p.strip() for p in header.split("|")]   
      456 +        loops = [self._parse_for_header(p) for p in loop_parts
          +]                                                             
      457 +        block: Block = loops[0] if len(loops) == 1 else Parall
          +el(loops=loops)                                               
      458 +        parent.append(block)                                  
      459 +        self._pos += 1                                        
      460 +        self._parse_statements(block, min_indent + 2)         
      461 +                                                              
      462 +    def _parse_for_header(self, header: str) -> Block:        
      463 +        """Parse a single ``for <var> in <generator>`` header 
          +into a loop block."""                                         
      464 +        m = _FOR_HEADER_RE.match(header.strip())              
      465 +        if not m:                                             
      466 +            msg = f"invalid for loop header: {header!r}"      
      467 +            raise ParseError(msg, self._pos + 1)              
      468 +        var_name = m.group(1)                                 
      469 +        var = self.get_or_declare_variable(var_name)          
      470 +        rest = m.group(2).strip()                             
      471 +        # List literal -> the registered ``values`` generator.
      472 +        if rest.startswith("["):                              
      473 +            spec = get_sweep_generator_spec("values")         
      474 +            if spec is None:                                  
      475 +                msg = "sweep generator 'values' is not registe
          +red"                                                          
      476 +                raise ParseError(msg, self._pos + 1)          
      477 +            return spec.parse(var, rest, self)                
      478 +        # Otherwise: ``name(...)`` — look up the name in the r
          +egistry.                                                      
      479 +        if "(" not in rest:                                   
      480 +            msg = f"unknown sweep source {rest!r}; expected `n
          +ame(args)` or `[values]`"                                     
      481 +            raise ParseError(msg, self._pos + 1)              
      482 +        paren = rest.index("(")                               
      483 +        gen_name = rest[:paren].strip()                       
      484 +        args_text = rest[paren + 1 : rest.rindex(")")]        
      485 +        spec = get_sweep_generator_spec(gen_name)             
      486 +        if spec is None:                                      
      487 +            msg = f"unknown sweep generator {gen_name!r}"     
      488 +            raise ParseError(msg, self._pos + 1)              
      489 +        return spec.parse(var, args_text, self)               
      490 +

      622 +    def _parse_paren_expression(self, tok: str) -> object:    
      623 +        """Parse a parenthesised symbolic expression token.   
      624 +                                                              
      625 +        Round-trips the forms emitted by the writer for :class
          +:`BinaryOp`                                                   
      626 +        and :class:`UnaryOp`:                                 
      627 +                                                              
      628 +        - Binary: ``(<left> <op> <right>)`` — spaces around th
          +e operator.                                                   
      629 +        - Unary:  ``(<op><operand>)`` — no space between op an
          +d operand.                                                    
      630 +                                                              
      631 +        Nested parens are respected when locating the top-leve
          +l operator,                                                   
      632 +        so deeper expressions like ``((a + b) * c)`` parse cor
          +rectly.                                                       
      633 +        """                                                   
      634 +        from qprogram.variable import BinaryOp, Constant, Unar
          +yOp  # noqa: PLC0415                                          
      635 +                                                              
      636 +        inner = tok[1:-1].strip()                             
      637 +        if not inner:                                         
      638 +            msg = f"empty expression: {tok!r}"                
      639 +            raise ParseError(msg, self._pos + 1)              
      640 +        # Unary form: ``+x`` or ``-x`` where there's no top-le
          +vel binary op.                                                
      641 +        # Detect by checking the leading sign and the absence 
          +of a top-level                                                
      642 +        # binary operator.                                    
      643 +        binary_op_index = _find_top_level_binary_op(inner)    
      644 +        if binary_op_index is None and inner[0] in {"+", "-"}:
      645 +            op_char = inner[0]                                
      646 +            operand_tok = inner[1:].strip()                   
      647 +            operand = self.parse_value(operand_tok)           
      648 +            return UnaryOp(op_char, _to_expression(operand))  
          +# type: ignore[arg-type]                                      
      649 +        if binary_op_index is None:                           
      650 +            msg = f"could not parse expression: {tok!r}"      
      651 +            raise ParseError(msg, self._pos + 1)              
      652 +        op_char = inner[binary_op_index]                      
      653 +        left_tok = inner[:binary_op_index].strip()            
      654 +        right_tok = inner[binary_op_index + 1 :].strip()      
      655 +        left = _to_expression(self.parse_value(left_tok))     
      656 +        right = _to_expression(self.parse_value(right_tok))   
      657 +        # The writer never emits operators outside ``+ - * /``
          +, so the cast                                                 
      658 +        # is safe at this point.                              
      659 +        if op_char not in {"+", "-", "*", "/"}:  # pragma: no 
          +cover — defensive                                             
      660 +            msg = f"unknown operator {op_char!r} in expression
          + {tok!r}"                                                     
      661 +            raise ParseError(msg, self._pos + 1)              
      662 +        # Strip unused import noise.                          
      663 +        _ = Constant                                          
      664 +        return BinaryOp(op_char, left, right)  # type: ignore[
          +arg-type]

  ⎿  Added 55 lines
      723      return val
      724  
      725  
      726 +def _find_top_level_binary_op(s: str) -> int | None:          
      727 +    """Locate the index of a top-level binary operator in ``s`
          +`.                                                            
      728 +                                                              
      729 +    A binary operator is one of ``+ - * /`` that has a space o
          +n at least one                                                
      730 +    side and is not inside any nested parens. Returns ``None``
          + if no such                                                   
      731 +    operator exists, in which case the caller treats ``s`` as 
          +a unary form                                                  
      732 +    (leading sign) or atom.                                   
      733 +    """                                                       
      734 +    depth = 0                                                 
      735 +    in_q = False                                              
      736 +    n = len(s)                                                
      737 +    for i, c in enumerate(s):                                 
      738 +        if c == '"':                                          
      739 +            in_q = not in_q                                   
      740 +            continue                                          
      741 +        if in_q:                                              
      742 +            continue                                          
      743 +        if c == "(":                                          
      744 +            depth += 1                                        
      745 +            continue                                          
      746 +        if c == ")":                                          
      747 +            depth -= 1                                        
      748 +            continue                                          
      749 +        if depth != 0:                                        
      750 +            continue                                          
      751 +        if c not in {"+", "-", "*", "/"}:                     
      752 +            continue                                          
      753 +        # Writer emits binary ops surrounded by spaces; the le
          +ading ``+``/``-``                                             
      754 +        # of a unary expression sits flush against its operand
          +. Require a                                                   
      755 +        # preceding space to disambiguate.                    
      756 +        if i == 0 or s[i - 1] != " ":                         
      757 +            continue                                          
      758 +        if i + 1 >= n or s[i + 1] != " ":                     
      759 +            continue                                          
      760 +        return i                                              
      761 +    return None                                               
      762 +                                                              
      763 +                                                              
      764 +def _to_expression(value: object) -> object:                  
      765 +    """Promote a parsed value into an :class:`Expression`-comp
          +atible operand.                                               
      766 +                                                              
      767 +    Numbers become :class:`Constant`; variables and expression
          + nodes pass                                                   
      768 +    through. Anything else (a bus string, say) reaches here on
          +ly as a result                                                
      769 +    of malformed input — pass it through and let the AST const
          +ructor                                                        
      770 +    complain.                                                 
      771 +    """                                                       
      772 +    from qprogram.variable import Constant, Expression  # noqa
          +: PLC0415                                                     
      773 +                                                              
      774 +    if isinstance(value, Expression):                         
      775 +        return value                                          
      776 +    if isinstance(value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(valu
          +e, bool):                                                     
      777 +        return Constant(value)                                
      778 +    return value                                              
      779 +

● Done. Quick summary of what changed:

  New / changed files:
  - qprogram/serialization/registry.py — added OperationSpec, BlockSpec,
  SweepGeneratorSpec dataclasses; new register_operation, register_block,
  register_sweep_generator APIs; register_vendor_operation is now an alias.
  - qprogram/serialization/_specs.py (new) — default signature-driven
  default_serialize_operation / default_parse_operation; special-case callbacks
  for sync (variadic), get_parameter (-> ident), set_crosstalk (stub); block
  header callbacks for average; sweep generator callbacks for range, values (the
   [...] literal), file; _register_core_specs() is invoked once on import.
  - qprogram/serialization/__init__.py — imports _specs to activate the dispatch
   table; exports register_block, register_operation, register_sweep_generator,
  and the spec dataclasses.
  - qprogram/serialization/writer.py — _serialize_operation is now a registry
  lookup; _write_block_contents dispatches via the block + sweep registries; the
   per-op isinstance ladder is gone. Operations are exposed via
  serialize_value/serialize_bus/serialize_waveform/var_ident as the
  write-context API.
  - qprogram/serialization/parser.py — _parse_operation is registry-driven;
  _try_parse_block_header dispatches by keyword via the block registry;
  _parse_for_header dispatches by generator name. Added parsing for
  parenthesised symbolic expressions (writer emitted them, parser previously
  couldn't read them back). Exposes
  parse_value/parse_error/get_or_declare_variable/declared_variable as the
  parse-context API. Removed dead helpers _parse_kwargs and _construct_generic.

  Behavior changes worth noting (pre-1.0, fine per the framing):
  - Optional parameters with non-default values are now serialized as key=value
  kwargs (e.g. set_offset "flux_q0" 0.1 offset_path1=0.2, qblox.set_trigger 
  "bus" 100 position="end"). The old qblox path emitted everything positional
  which broke for None defaults; that's fixed.
  - Arithmetic expressions like (freq + 1e6) now round-trip — they were emitted
  but unparseable before.

  Validation:
  - ruff passes on both packages.
  - ty: 31 diagnostics (down from 32 pre-refactor); all remaining are
  pre-existing.
  - Round-trip on a comprehensive program (schema-backed buses, parallel
  for_loop | loop, nested for and block, every core op, every qblox op,
  expressions, get_parameter arrow, variadic sync, save_adc, optional kwargs) is
   byte-stable.

  Extension points now in place for future tasks:
  - Adding if_ / repeat blocks: one register_block(...) call each.
  - Adding linspace / logspace sweeps: one register_sweep_generator(...) call
  each.
  - Adding new core operations (e.g. classify): one register_operation(...)
  call; default signature-driven serialize/parse handles it for free.
  - Vendor packages keep using register_vendor_operation exactly as before — no
  changes needed on the qblox side.

❯ Let's continue with implementing "3. Widen Expression AST to include          
  comparison/logical/conditional/math node types (§3)." Explain what you will   
  implement before implementing.                                                

● Before writing code, here's the plan — it has a few real design choices, so I
  want to flag them before committing.

  What's getting added

  Five new AST node classes in qprogram/variable.py, plus operator overloads and
   module-level helpers. The new tree looks like:

  Expression (existing ABC)
  ├─ Variable                    (existing — leaf, identity-based eq, mutable
  value)
  ├─ Constant                    (existing — leaf, structural eq)
  ├─ BinaryOp     + - * /        (existing)
  ├─ UnaryOp      + -            (existing)
  ├─ Comparison   == != < <= > >=         ← new
  ├─ LogicalBinaryOp   and or              ← new
  ├─ LogicalNot   not                       ← new
  ├─ MathFunc     sin cos tan exp log sqrt abs minimum maximum   ← new (one
  class, named op)
  └─ Where        ternary select            ← new

  All five new classes follow the existing pattern: structural __eq__/__hash__,
  evaluate() propagates UNASSIGNED, variables() is the recursive union.

  How users build them (Python API)

  For arithmetic the operators stay the same. For the new ops:

  ┌────────────────────┬───────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
  │     Construct      │     Python syntax     │           Builds           │
  ├────────────────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Comparison (<, <=, │ freq < 5e9            │ Comparison("<", freq,      │
  │  >, >=)            │                       │ Constant(5e9))             │
  ├────────────────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Equality (==, !=)  │ qp.eq(freq, 5e9) /    │ Comparison("==", freq,     │
  │                    │ qp.ne(...)            │ Constant(5e9))             │
  ├────────────────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Logical and/or/not │ (a < b) & (c > d), |, │ LogicalBinaryOp /          │
  │                    │  ~p                   │ LogicalNot                 │
  ├────────────────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Math functions     │ qp.sin(phase), abs(x) │ MathFunc("sin", phase)     │
  │                    │                       │ etc.                       │
  ├────────────────────┼───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Conditional        │ qp.where(cond, then,  │ Where(...)                 │
  │                    │ else_)                │                            │
  └────────────────────┴───────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

  Why eq/ne are functions, not operators: Python's __eq__ must return a bool for
   sets/dicts to work, and expression.variables() already returns set[Variable].
   Overriding == to build a Comparison would break that machinery. Free
  functions sidestep the issue cleanly — same approach SymPy takes.

  Why &, |, ~ for logical: and/or/not are Python keywords (short-circuit, can't
  be overloaded). &/|/~ are the conventional substitutes (NumPy, Pandas, SymPy
  all use these). Module-level qp.and_/qp.or_/qp.not_ are also provided for
  users who prefer named functions.

  abs(x) works via Expression.__abs__. min/max would silently misbehave because
  Python's builtins use < which would now return a (truthy) Comparison. So we
  expose qp.minimum(a, b) and qp.maximum(a, b) instead (matches NumPy).

  Serialization format

  Round-trip via the existing parenthesized/function-call patterns the writer
  and parser already understand:

  ┌─────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │      Node       │                       .qp syntax                       │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Comparison      │ (freq < 5000000000.0), (a == b), (a != b), (a <= b),   │
  │                 │ (a >= b), (a > b)                                      │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ LogicalBinaryOp │ (p and q), (p or q)                                    │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ LogicalNot      │ (not p)                                                │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ MathFunc        │ sin(phase), minimum(a, b), abs(x)                      │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Where           │ where(cond, then, else)                                │
  └─────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Comparisons and binary logical ops reuse the existing (<left> <op> <right>)
  shape; the parser's expression-token splitter just needs to recognize the new
  operator names. MathFunc and Where use the function-call shape that already
  exists for waveforms — dispatch by name in the parser.

  Three things I want to confirm before coding

● User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · Should `bool(expression)` raise TypeError to catch accidental use in 
     `if`/`assert`/`and`/`or`? → Raise TypeError (SymPy-style)
     · How should `LogicalBinaryOp` evaluate when one operand is UNASSIGNED? → 
     Always propagate UNASSIGNED (recommended)
     · Where should the math/conditional helpers (`sin`, `where`, `eq`, `and_`, 
     etc.) be exposed? → Top-level qprogram package (recommended)
