      483 +class Comparison(Expression):                                 
      484 +    """Binary comparison node: ``left op right`` where ``op`` 
          +is one of                                                     
      485 +    ``== != < <= > >=``.                                      
      486 +                                                              
      487 +    Constructed by the comparison operators on :class:`Express
          +ion` (``<``,                                                  
      488 +    ``<=``, ``>``, ``>=``) and by the :func:`~qprogram.eq` /  
      489 +    :func:`~qprogram.ne` helpers (``==``, ``!=``; see :class:`
          +Expression`                                                   
      490 +    for why these are not overloaded directly).               
      491 +                                                              
      492 +    ``evaluate()`` returns ``bool`` (which Python treats as an
          + ``int``                                                      
      493 +    subclass, hence the broader return annotation on the base 
          +class), or                                                    
      494 +    :data:`UNASSIGNED` if either operand is unassigned.       
      495 +    """                                                       
      496 +                                                              
      497 +    _OPS: ClassVar[frozenset[str]] = frozenset({"==", "!=", "<
          +", "<=", ">", ">="})                                          
      498 +                                                              
      499 +    def __init__(self, op: ComparisonOperator, left: Expressio
          +n, right: Expression) -> None:                                
      500 +        if op not in self._OPS:  # defensive: callers should p
          +ass a Literal                                                 
      501 +            msg = f"Comparison op must be one of {sorted(self.
          +_OPS)}, got {op!r}"                                           
      502 +            raise ValueError(msg)                             
      503 +        self.op: ComparisonOperator = op                      
      504 +        self.left: Expression = left                          
      505 +        self.right: Expression = right                        
      506 +                                                              
      507 +    def evaluate(self) -> bool | _UnassignedType:             
      508 +        left_value = self.left.evaluate()                     
      509 +        if isinstance(left_value, _UnassignedType):           
      510 +            return UNASSIGNED                                 
      511 +        right_value = self.right.evaluate()                   
      512 +        if isinstance(right_value, _UnassignedType):          
      513 +            return UNASSIGNED                                 
      514 +        if self.op == "==":                                   
      515 +            return left_value == right_value                  
      516 +        if self.op == "!=":                                   
      517 +            return left_value != right_value                  
      518 +        if self.op == "<":                                    
      519 +            return left_value < right_value                   
      520 +        if self.op == "<=":                                   
      521 +            return left_value <= right_value                  
      522 +        if self.op == ">":                                    
      523 +            return left_value > right_value                   
      524 +        return left_value >= right_value                      
      525 +                                                              
      526 +    def variables(self) -> set[Variable]:                     
      527 +        return self.left.variables() | self.right.variables() 
      528 +                                                              
      529 +    def __hash__(self) -> int:                                
      530 +        return hash(("Comparison", self.op, self.left, self.ri
          +ght))                                                         
      531 +                                                              
      532 +    def __eq__(self, other: object) -> bool:                  
      533 +        return (                                              
      534 +            isinstance(other, Comparison)                     
      535 +            and self.op == other.op                           
      536 +            and self.left == other.left                       
      537 +            and self.right == other.right                     
      538 +        )                                                     
      539 +                                                              
      540 +    def __repr__(self) -> str:                                
      541 +        return f"({self.left} {self.op} {self.right})"        
      542 +                                                              
      543 +                                                              
      544 +class LogicalBinaryOp(Expression):                            
      545 +    """Binary logical node: ``left op right`` where ``op`` is 
          +``and`` or ``or``.                                            
      546 +                                                              
      547 +    Constructed by ``&`` and ``|`` on :class:`Expression`, or 
          +by the                                                        
      548 +    :func:`~qprogram.and_` / :func:`~qprogram.or_` module help
          +ers.                                                          
      549 +                                                              
      550 +    Evaluation never short-circuits — any :data:`UNASSIGNED` o
          +perand                                                        
      551 +    propagates :data:`UNASSIGNED` upwards. This matches the re
          +st of the                                                     
      552 +    evaluator (``BinaryOp``, ``Comparison``, ``UnaryOp``) and 
          +makes                                                         
      553 +    unbound-variable diagnostics deterministic instead of posi
          +tion-dependent.                                               
      554 +    """                                                       
      555 +                                                              
      556 +    _OPS: ClassVar[frozenset[str]] = frozenset({"and", "or"}) 
      557 +                                                              
      558 +    def __init__(self, op: LogicalBinaryOperator, left: Expres
          +sion, right: Expression) -> None:                             
      559 +        if op not in self._OPS:  # defensive                  
      560 +            msg = f"LogicalBinaryOp op must be one of {sorted(
          +self._OPS)}, got {op!r}"                                      
      561 +            raise ValueError(msg)                             
      562 +        self.op: LogicalBinaryOperator = op                   
      563 +        self.left: Expression = left                          
      564 +        self.right: Expression = right                        
      565 +                                                              
      566 +    def evaluate(self) -> bool | _UnassignedType:             
      567 +        left_value = self.left.evaluate()                     
      568 +        if isinstance(left_value, _UnassignedType):           
      569 +            return UNASSIGNED                                 
      570 +        right_value = self.right.evaluate()                   
      571 +        if isinstance(right_value, _UnassignedType):          
      572 +            return UNASSIGNED                                 
      573 +        if self.op == "and":                                  
      574 +            return bool(left_value) and bool(right_value)     
      575 +        return bool(left_value) or bool(right_value)          
      576 +                                                              
      577 +    def variables(self) -> set[Variable]:                     
      578 +        return self.left.variables() | self.right.variables() 
      579 +                                                              
      580 +    def __hash__(self) -> int:                                
      581 +        return hash(("LogicalBinaryOp", self.op, self.left, se
          +lf.right))                                                    
      582 +                                                              
      583 +    def __eq__(self, other: object) -> bool:                  
      584 +        return (                                              
      585 +            isinstance(other, LogicalBinaryOp)                
      586 +            and self.op == other.op                           
      587 +            and self.left == other.left                       
      588 +            and self.right == other.right                     
      589 +        )                                                     
      590 +                                                              
      591 +    def __repr__(self) -> str:                                
      592 +        return f"({self.left} {self.op} {self.right})"        
      593 +                                                              
      594 +                                                              
      595 +class LogicalNot(Expression):                                 
      596 +    """Logical negation node: ``not operand``.                
      597 +                                                              
      598 +    Constructed by ``~`` on :class:`Expression` or by         
      599 +    :func:`~qprogram.not_`.                                   
      600 +    """                                                       
      601 +                                                              
      602 +    def __init__(self, operand: Expression) -> None:          
      603 +        self.operand: Expression = operand                    
      604 +                                                              
      605 +    def evaluate(self) -> bool | _UnassignedType:             
      606 +        operand_value = self.operand.evaluate()               
      607 +        if isinstance(operand_value, _UnassignedType):        
      608 +            return UNASSIGNED                                 
      609 +        return not bool(operand_value)                        
      610 +                                                              
      611 +    def variables(self) -> set[Variable]:                     
      612 +        return self.operand.variables()                       
      613 +                                                              
      614 +    def __hash__(self) -> int:                                
      615 +        return hash(("LogicalNot", self.operand))             
      616 +                                                              
      617 +    def __eq__(self, other: object) -> bool:                  
      618 +        return isinstance(other, LogicalNot) and self.operand 
          +== other.operand                                              
      619 +                                                              
      620 +    def __repr__(self) -> str:                                
      621 +        return f"(not {self.operand})"                        
      622 +                                                              
      623 +                                                              
      624 +# ------------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      625 +# Math functions and conditional                              
      626 +# ------------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      627 +                                                              
      628 +                                                              
      629 +class MathFunc(Expression):                                   
      630 +    """Math function application: ``name(arg1, arg2, ...)``.  
      631 +                                                              
      632 +    Carries a function name plus a tuple of operand :class:`Ex
          +pression`                                                     
      633 +    instances. The registered name determines arity (e.g. ``si
          +n`` takes one                                                 
      634 +    argument, ``minimum`` takes two) and the numeric evaluator
          +.                                                             
      635 +                                                              
      636 +    Built-in names are listed in :data:`_MATH_FUNCTIONS`. Each
          + is dispatched                                                
      637 +    via the lazy lookup in :func:`_math_eval`, keeping this mo
          +dule free of a                                                
      638 +    direct ``numpy``/``math`` import at the AST layer.        
      639 +    """                                                       
      640 +                                                              
      641 +    def __init__(self, name: str, operands: tuple[Expression, 
          +...]) -> None:                                                
      642 +        if name not in _MATH_FUNCTIONS:                       
      643 +            msg = f"Unknown math function {name!r}; known: {so
          +rted(_MATH_FUNCTIONS)}"                                       
      644 +            raise ValueError(msg)                             
      645 +        if not operands:                                      
      646 +            msg = f"Math function {name!r} requires at least o
          +ne operand"                                                   
      647 +            raise ValueError(msg)                             
      648 +        self.name: str = name                                 
      649 +        self.operands: tuple[Expression, ...] = operands      
      650 +                                                              
      651 +    def evaluate(self) -> int | float | _UnassignedType:      
      652 +        values: list[int | float] = []                        
      653 +        for op in self.operands:                              
      654 +            v = op.evaluate()                                 
      655 +            if isinstance(v, _UnassignedType):                
      656 +                return UNASSIGNED                             
      657 +            values.append(v)                                  
      658 +        return _math_eval(self.name, values)                  
      659 +                                                              
      660 +    def variables(self) -> set[Variable]:                     
      661 +        out: set[Variable] = set()                            
      662 +        for op in self.operands:                              
      663 +            out |= op.variables()                             
      664 +        return out                                            
      665 +                                                              
      666 +    def __hash__(self) -> int:                                
      667 +        return hash(("MathFunc", self.name, self.operands))   
      668 +                                                              
      669 +    def __eq__(self, other: object) -> bool:                  
      670 +        return (                                              
      671 +            isinstance(other, MathFunc)                       
      672 +            and self.name == other.name                       
      673 +            and self.operands == other.operands               
      674 +        )                                                     
      675 +                                                              
      676 +    def __repr__(self) -> str:                                
      677 +        args = ", ".join(repr(op) for op in self.operands)    
      678 +        return f"{self.name}({args})"                         
      679 +                                                              
      680 +                                                              
      681 +class Where(Expression):                                      
      682 +    """Ternary conditional: ``where(condition, then, else_)``.
      683 +                                                              
      684 +    Evaluates ``condition``; if true, returns ``then``'s value
          +, otherwise                                                   
      685 +    ``else_``'s. The unchosen branch is not evaluated, so it m
          +ay reference                                                  
      686 +    unassigned variables without forcing the whole expression 
          +to                                                            
      687 +    :data:`UNASSIGNED`. If the ``condition`` itself is unassig
          +ned, the                                                      
      688 +    whole expression evaluates to :data:`UNASSIGNED`.         
      689 +                                                              
      690 +    Construct with the module-level :func:`~qprogram.where` he
          +lper.                                                         
      691 +    """                                                       
      692 +                                                              
      693 +    def __init__(self, condition: Expression, then: Expression
          +, else_: Expression) -> None:                                 
      694 +        self.condition: Expression = condition                
      695 +        self.then: Expression = then                          
      696 +        self.else_: Expression = else_                        
      697 +                                                              
      698 +    def evaluate(self) -> int | float | _UnassignedType:      
      699 +        cond_value = self.condition.evaluate()                
      700 +        if isinstance(cond_value, _UnassignedType):           
      701 +            return UNASSIGNED                                 
      702 +        chosen = self.then if cond_value else self.else_      
      703 +        return chosen.evaluate()                              
      704 +                                                              
      705 +    def variables(self) -> set[Variable]:                     
      706 +        return self.condition.variables() | self.then.variable
          +s() | self.else_.variables()                                  
      707 +                                                              
      708 +    def __hash__(self) -> int:                                
      709 +        return hash(("Where", self.condition, self.then, self.
          +else_))                                                       
      710 +                                                              
      711 +    def __eq__(self, other: object) -> bool:                  
      712 +        return (                                              
      713 +            isinstance(other, Where)                          
      714 +            and self.condition == other.condition             
      715 +            and self.then == other.then                       
      716 +            and self.else_ == other.else_                     
      717 +        )                                                     
      718 +                                                              
      719 +    def __repr__(self) -> str:                                
      720 +        return f"where({self.condition!r}, {self.then!r}, {sel
          +f.else_!r})"                                                  
      721 +                                                              
      722 +                                                              
      723 +# ------------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      724 +# Math function evaluator (lazy numpy import)                 
      725 +# ------------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      726 +                                                              
      727 +                                                              
      728 +def _math_eval(name: str, values: list[int | float]) -> int | 
          +float:                                                        
      729 +    """Dispatch :class:`MathFunc` evaluation.                 
      730 +                                                              
      731 +    Imports :mod:`numpy` lazily so that constructing :class:`M
          +athFunc`                                                      
      732 +    instances at AST-construction time is free; we only pay th
          +e import when                                                 
      733 +    a tree is actually evaluated. Returns Python scalars (``fl
          +oat`` for                                                     
      734 +    transcendental functions; ``abs`` preserves the integer-vs
          +-float                                                        
      735 +    distinction).                                             
      736 +    """                                                       
      737 +    import numpy as np  # noqa: PLC0415                       
      738 +                                                              
      739 +    if name == "abs":                                         
      740 +        return abs(values[0])                                 
      741 +    if name == "minimum":                                     
      742 +        return min(values)                                    
      743 +    if name == "maximum":                                     
      744 +        return max(values)                                    
      745 +    fn = {                                                    
      746 +        "sin": np.sin,                                        
      747 +        "cos": np.cos,                                        
      748 +        "tan": np.tan,                                        
      749 +        "exp": np.exp,                                        
      750 +        "log": np.log,                                        
      751 +        "sqrt": np.sqrt,                                      
      752 +    }[name]                                                   
      753 +    return float(fn(values[0]))                               
      754 +                                                              
      755 +                                                              
      756 +# ------------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      757 +# Module-level builders for ops Python syntax can't reach     
      758 +# ------------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      759 +                                                              
      760 +                                                              
      761 +def eq(left: Expression | float, right: Expression | float) ->
          + Comparison:                                                  
      762 +    """Build an equality comparison ``left == right``.        
      763 +                                                              
      764 +    Used instead of overloading ``==`` so that :class:`Variabl
          +e`'s                                                          
      765 +    identity-based equality (needed for sets / dict keys / AST
          + traversal)                                                   
      766 +    keeps working. The right-hand side is coerced from int/flo
          +at via                                                        
      767 +    :class:`Constant`.                                        
      768 +    """                                                       
      769 +    return Comparison("==", _wrap(left), _wrap(right))        
      770 +                                                              
      771 +                                                              
      772 +def ne(left: Expression | float, right: Expression | float) ->
          + Comparison:                                                  
      773 +    """Build an inequality comparison ``left != right`` (count
          +erpart of :func:`eq`)."""                                     
      774 +    return Comparison("!=", _wrap(left), _wrap(right))        
      775 +                                                              
      776 +                                                              
      777 +def and_(left: Expression, right: Expression) -> LogicalBinary
          +Op:                                                           
      778 +    """Build ``left and right`` — function form of the ``&`` o
          +perator.                                                      
      779 +                                                              
      780 +    Useful when chaining or when callers find ``and_(p, q)`` c
          +learer than                                                   
      781 +    ``p & q``. Precedence reminder: ``&`` binds tighter than t
          +he comparison                                                 
      782 +    operators in Python, so ``a < b & c < d`` parses as ``a < 
          +(b & c) < d``;                                                
      783 +    parenthesise comparisons explicitly: ``(a < b) & (c < d)``
          +.                                                             
      784 +    """                                                       
      785 +    return LogicalBinaryOp("and", left, right)                
      786 +                                                              
      787 +                                                              
      788 +def or_(left: Expression, right: Expression) -> LogicalBinaryO
          +p:                                                            
      789 +    """Build ``left or right`` — function form of the ``|`` op
          +erator."""                                                    
      790 +    return LogicalBinaryOp("or", left, right)                 
      791 +                                                              
      792 +                                                              
      793 +def not_(operand: Expression) -> LogicalNot:                  
      794 +    """Build ``not operand`` — function form of the ``~`` oper
          +ator."""                                                      
      795 +    return LogicalNot(operand)                                
      796 +                                                              
      797 +                                                              
      798 +def sin(x: Expression | float) -> MathFunc:                   
      799 +    """Build ``sin(x)``."""                                   
      800 +    return MathFunc("sin", (_wrap(x),))                       
      801 +                                                              
      802 +                                                              
      803 +def cos(x: Expression | float) -> MathFunc:                   
      804 +    """Build ``cos(x)``."""                                   
      805 +    return MathFunc("cos", (_wrap(x),))                       
      806 +                                                              
      807 +                                                              
      808 +def tan(x: Expression | float) -> MathFunc:                   
      809 +    """Build ``tan(x)``."""                                   
      810 +    return MathFunc("tan", (_wrap(x),))                       
      811 +                                                              
      812 +                                                              
      813 +def exp(x: Expression | float) -> MathFunc:                   
      814 +    """Build ``exp(x)``."""                                   
      815 +    return MathFunc("exp", (_wrap(x),))                       
      816 +                                                              
      817 +                                                              
      818 +def log(x: Expression | float) -> MathFunc:                   
      819 +    """Build ``log(x)`` (natural logarithm)."""               
      820 +    return MathFunc("log", (_wrap(x),))                       
      821 +                                                              
      822 +                                                              
      823 +def sqrt(x: Expression | float) -> MathFunc:                  
      824 +    """Build ``sqrt(x)``."""                                  
      825 +    return MathFunc("sqrt", (_wrap(x),))                      
      826 +                                                              
      827 +                                                              
      828 +def minimum(*args: Expression | float) -> MathFunc:           
      829 +    """Build ``minimum(a, b, ...)`` — the symbolic counterpart
          + of Python's :func:`min`.                                     
      830 +                                                              
      831 +    A separate name is required because the built-in :func:`mi
          +n` uses ``<``                                                 
      832 +    for comparison, which on an :class:`Expression` builds a  
      833 +    :class:`Comparison` node (truthy), causing ``min(var_a, va
          +r_b)`` to                                                     
      834 +    silently return ``var_a`` rather than build the symbolic m
          +inimum.                                                       
      835 +    """                                                       
      836 +    if len(args) < 2:                                         
      837 +        msg = "minimum() requires at least two arguments"     
      838 +        raise TypeError(msg)                                  
      839 +    return MathFunc("minimum", tuple(_wrap(a) for a in args)) 
      840 +                                                              
      841 +                                                              
      842 +def maximum(*args: Expression | float) -> MathFunc:           
      843 +    """Build ``maximum(a, b, ...)``; see :func:`minimum` for t
          +he rationale."""                                              
      844 +    if len(args) < 2:                                         
      845 +        msg = "maximum() requires at least two arguments"     
      846 +        raise TypeError(msg)                                  
      847 +    return MathFunc("maximum", tuple(_wrap(a) for a in args)) 
      848 +                                                              
      849 +                                                              
      850 +def where(condition: Expression, then: Expression | float, els
          +e_: Expression | float) -> Where:                             
      851 +    """Build a ternary ``where(condition, then, else_)`` expre
          +ssion.                                                        
      852 +                                                              
      853 +    The condition must be an :class:`Expression` (typically a 
      854 +    :class:`Comparison` or :class:`LogicalBinaryOp`); the bran
          +ches may be                                                   
      855 +    expressions or numeric literals, which are wrapped as :cla
          +ss:`Constant`.                                                
      856 +    Only the chosen branch is evaluated, so the unchosen branc
          +h may                                                         
      857 +    reference variables that happen to be unassigned at evalua
          +tion time.                                                    
      858 +    """                                                       
      859 +    return Where(condition, _wrap(then), _wrap(else_))        
      860 +                                                              
      861 +                                                              
      862 +# ------------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      863  # Helpers
      864  # ------------------------------------------------------------

      687 +    def _parse_function_call(self, tok: str) -> object:       
      688 +        """Dispatch a ``name(args)`` token to math, ``where``,
          + or waveform.                                                 
      689 +

          +`, ``minimum``,

          + are

          +ing else is

          +c, Where  # noqa: PLC0415                                     
      696 +

      698 +        name = tok[:paren_idx]                                
      699 +        args_text = tok[paren_idx + 1 : tok.rindex(")")]      
      700 +        if name in _MATH_FUNCTIONS:

          +())) for part in _split_args(args_text)]

          +[arg-type]

          +p())) for part in _split_args(args_text)]

          +tion, then, else); got {len(parts)}"                          
      707 +                raise ParseError(msg, self._pos + 1)          
      708 +            return Where(parts[0], parts[1], parts[2])  # type
          +: ignore[arg-type]                                            
      709 +        return _parse_waveform_expr(tok)

      886 +def _require_expression(value: object, *, where: str) -> None:
      887 +    """Raise :class:`TypeError` if ``value`` is not an :class:
          +`Expression`.                                                 
      888 +                                                              
      889 +    Used by node constructors that take an :class:`Expression`
          +-typed operand                                                
      890 +    *without* numeric coercion (logical operators, ``Where`` c
          +ondition). The                                                
      891 +    most common pitfall is writing ``var == literal`` and expe
          +cting a                                                       
      892 +    :class:`Comparison` — but ``Variable.__eq__`` is identity-
          +based, so it                                                  
      893 +    returns ``bool``. The error message points users to the ri
          +ght helper.                                                   
      894 +    """                                                       
      895 +    if isinstance(value, Expression):                         
      896 +        return                                                
      897 +    hint = ""                                                 
      898 +    if isinstance(value, bool):                               
      899 +        hint = (                                              
      900 +            " — if you wrote `var == literal` or `var != liter
          +al`, use "                                                    
      901 +            "qprogram.eq(var, literal) / qprogram.ne(...) inst
          +ead, since "                                                  
      902 +            "Variable's `==` returns identity-based bool, not 
          +a Comparison."                                                
      903 +        )                                                     
      904 +    msg = f"{where} must be an Expression; got {type(value).__
          +name__}{hint}"                                                
      905 +    raise TypeError(msg)

● Done. Summary:

  Files touched:
  - qprogram/variable.py — added 5 AST classes (Comparison, LogicalBinaryOp,
  LogicalNot, MathFunc, Where), comparison + logical operator overloads on
  Expression, __bool__ guard, __abs__, module-level builders (eq, ne, and_, or_,
   not_, sin, cos, tan, exp, log, sqrt, minimum, maximum, where),
  _require_expression validator, lazy _math_eval numpy dispatcher.
  - qprogram/__init__.py — re-exported the new classes and helpers.
  - qprogram/serialization/writer.py — serialize_value now handles Comparison,
  LogicalBinaryOp, LogicalNot (parenthesized form, same as BinaryOp) and
  MathFunc/Where (function-call form, same shape waveforms use).
  - qprogram/serialization/parser.py — _parse_paren_expression rewritten to
  tokenize the inner expression and dispatch by token count and middle-operator
  name; new _parse_function_call method routes sin(...)/where(...) to AST nodes,
   falls through to the waveform registry. Removed dead
  _find_top_level_binary_op.

  Behavior verified:
  - Construction for every new node type — comparison, logical (operator and
  named-function forms), math, where.
  - evaluate() with bound and unbound variables (UNASSIGNED propagates
  uniformly; Where short-circuits unchosen branch).
  - __bool__ guard fires on if expr: with a helpful error.
  - Constructor type guard catches the common where(var == lit, …) mistake and
  points to qp.eq(...).
  - Round-trip stable for a program touching every new node plus
  arithmetic/sin/abs/minimum/maximum/where/and_/or_/not_/eq/ne.
  - Prior registry-refactor regression test (schema, parallel loops, vendor ops,
   get_parameter arrow, sync variadic, save_adc kwargs) still byte-stable.

  Lint & type:
  - ruff clean on both packages.
  - ty clean (0 diagnostics; was 31 pre-task).

  Design decisions baked in (per the confirmed answers):
  - __bool__ raises — SymPy-style. Catches if expr: bugs.
  - Logical/comparison evaluate fully (no short-circuit) so UNASSIGNED
  propagates deterministically.
  - All new helpers live at qp.<name> for discoverability.

  Two intentional limitations worth noting (for follow-ups, not blockers):
  1. Math functions and other expression nodes are not yet parseable inside
  waveform constructor arguments (Gaussian(amplitude=sin(amp))). The
  waveform-args parser path (_parse_arg) doesn't yet share the operation-args
  dispatch (parse_value). Variables already had this limitation, and unifying
  the two is a separate small refactor.
  2. Constant still rejects bool literals (preserves the numeric-only
  invariant). True & expr doesn't work via operator dispatch; users write (a < 
  b) & (c < d) with both operands as expressions. Documented in the operator's
  comment.

  Foundations now in place for future tasks that the brainstorm called out:
  - A classify(measurement) -> Variable could land at any time; its result would
   feed where(eq(state, 1), …) or program.if_(eq(state, 1)) without any AST
  changes.
  - Adding dtype metadata to Variable becomes purely additive — every consumer
  of Expression is already factored through evaluate()/variables().
  - A future program.if_(predicate) block needs only block-registry
  registration; the predicate type is already representable.

❯ Let's move to "4. measure() returns a MeasurementHandle (§4)." What is your   
  proposal on this?                                                             

● Before coding, here's the proposal. I checked the result.py side first since
  the handle is the bridge between construction and result-retrieval, then
  thought through the shape and the corner cases.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)
● Now I have the full picture. Here's the proposal.

  Why we need this

  Today every measure() returns None and the result API is purely positional:

  program.measure(q[0].readout, "readout", "weights")   # returns None
  program.measure(q[1].readout, "readout", "weights")
  ...
  result.get(measurement=0)   # the first one — by *position* in source order

  Two problems compound the moment we want to do anything beyond toy programs:

  1. Brittle indexing. Inserting a measurement two lines up renumbers every
  downstream get(measurement=N). Refactors that should be local become global.
  2. No anchor for classification or feedback. When classify(handle, threshold)
  or program.if_(state == 1) lands (a known direction from the brainstorm), it
  has to point at a specific measurement. With no return value today, every
  measure call site would have to be rewritten.

  Biting the bullet now — pre-1.0, no consumers — is much cheaper than
  retrofitting later.

  The shape

  MeasurementHandle

  A lightweight value object — name plus structural equality:

  class MeasurementHandle:
      def __init__(self, name: str): self.name = name
      def __eq__(self, other): return isinstance(other, MeasurementHandle) and
  self.name == other.name
      def __hash__(self): return hash(("MeasurementHandle", self.name))
      def __repr__(self): return f"MeasurementHandle({self.name!r})"

  Lives in qprogram/result.py alongside QProgramResult (handles bridge
  construction → results; they belong with the result API).

  Measure carries a name

  class Measure(MeasurementOperation):    # new marker base
      def __init__(self, bus, waveform, weights, name: str, save_adc: bool = 
  False):
          self.bus = bus
          self.waveform = waveform
          self.weights = weights
          self.name = name
          self.save_adc = save_adc

  name is required on the AST node. The Python API auto-generates it when the
  user doesn't pass one.

  MeasurementOperation marker base

  A new empty subclass of Operation whose presence signals "this op produces a
  measurement and has a .name attribute." Lets the QProgram walk the AST to find
   used names without isinstance(op, Measure) or isinstance(op, Acquire) or ....
   Vendor extensions opt in by subclassing it.

  class MeasurementOperation(Operation):
      """Marker: produces a referenceable measurement; subclasses must expose 
  ``name: str``."""
      name: str   # contract

  Python API

  m0 = program.measure(q[0].readout, "readout", "weights")
  # -> MeasurementHandle(name="m0")

  m_rabi = program.measure(q[0].readout, "readout", "weights", name="rabi_pt")
  # -> MeasurementHandle(name="rabi_pt")

  program.measure(q[1].readout, "readout", "weights")   # discarding return is 
  fine

  - Auto-naming: m0, m1, m2, … by walking the AST for already-used names (no
  counter — survives deepcopy, with_waveforms, and round-trip without any extra
  bookkeeping field).
  - Explicit name= is checked against the existing set, raises ValueError on
  collision.

  Vendor measurement ops

  qblox.acquire(...) returns a MeasurementHandle too, sharing the same name pool
   with core measure. A small helper on VendorNamespace keeps vendor code clean:

  class VendorNamespace:
      def _append_measurement(self, op_cls, *, name=None, **kwargs) ->
  MeasurementHandle:
          allocated = self._program._allocate_measurement_name(name)
          self._append(op_cls(name=allocated, **kwargs))
          return MeasurementHandle(allocated)

  class QbloxNamespace:
      def acquire(self, bus, weights, save_adc=False, *, name=None) ->
  MeasurementHandle:
          return self._append_measurement(Acquire, bus=bus, weights=weights,
  save_adc=save_adc, name=name)

  Acquire becomes MeasurementOperation and gains a name field.

  ActiveReset is out of scope. It's a complex orchestration whose
  measurement-ness is interleaved with a conditional reset pulse — wrapping it
  in a MeasurementHandle cleanly is its own design choice. For now it stays
  as-is; a follow-up task can revisit when (or if) we want classification on its
   readout.

  Result API

  QProgramResult.get() accepts the handle, the name string, or a positional
  index (the latter kept as a one-version sugar so existing code keeps working):

  result.get(m0)            # MeasurementHandle — primary form
  result.get("m0")          # bare string — same lookup
  result.get(0)             # positional — sugar, falls through to handle list
  result.get(bus="q0/readout", measurement="m0")   # bus filter still works

  Internally MeasurementResult gains a name field; lookup walks the list by name
   when the argument is a handle or string.

  .qp serialization

  The name is always emitted so round-trip preserves handle identity. The
  default-spec rule says required positional → positional in the file:

  measure q[0].readout "readout" "weights" "m0"
  measure q[0].readout "readout" "weights" "rabi_pt"
  measure q[0].readout "readout" "weights" "m0" save_adc=true
  qblox.acquire q[0].readout "weights" "m1"
  qblox.acquire q[0].readout "weights" "m1" save_adc=true

  The parser default does the right thing automatically — name is a required
  positional in __init__, so the registry-driven dispatch reads it back into the
   field. No custom parse callback needed for this.

  Post-load handle reconstruction

  After qp.loads(text), the program is structurally complete but the original 
  handle objects are gone (they were Python locals on the writer side). Two
  complementary recovery paths:

  loaded = qp.loads(text)

  # 1) Re-derive a handle from the name if you know it:
  m = MeasurementHandle("m0")
  result.get(m)

  # 2) Discover names from the loaded program:
  loaded.measurement_handles()    # -> [MeasurementHandle("m0"), 
  MeasurementHandle("m1"), ...]

  A QProgram.measurement_handles() accessor walks the AST and rebuilds handles
  in declaration order. This is the load-time mirror of capturing return values
  at construction.

  What changes, file by file

  File: qprogram/result.py
  Change: Add MeasurementHandle; add name to MeasurementResult; widen
    QProgramResult.get to accept handle/str/int and append_measurement to take a

    name.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: qprogram/operations/operation.py
  Change: Add MeasurementOperation marker subclass.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: qprogram/operations/measure.py
  Change: Subclass MeasurementOperation; add required name field.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: qprogram/qprogram.py
  Change: measure() returns MeasurementHandle, takes optional name=; add
    _allocate_measurement_name(requested=None) walking AST; add
    measurement_handles() accessor.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: qprogram/vendor.py
  Change: Add _append_measurement(op_cls, *, name, **kwargs) -> 
    MeasurementHandle.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: qprogram-qblox/operations.py
  Change: Acquire → MeasurementOperation, adds name.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: qprogram-qblox/namespace.py
  Change: acquire() returns handle, takes optional name=.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: qprogram/__init__.py
  Change: Re-export MeasurementHandle.

  No changes needed in _specs.py or the writer/parser — Measure/Acquire go
  through the default-spec dispatch path, and name as a required positional Just
   Works.

  Out of scope (deliberate)

  - ActiveReset handle return — its dual-purpose semantics deserve a separate
  decision.
  - Classification (classify(handle)) — depends on this, but is its own task.
  - Conditional blocks (program.if_(...)) — likewise.
  - Per-shot vs averaged result shape — not affected by this change; covered by
  a future task.

  Decisions worth confirming

● User declined to answer questions

     identifiers? (Any non-empty string (recommended) / Identifier-only

  - the scope — e.g. should ActiveReset get the same handle treatment, or stay

✻ Worked for 2m 28s

❯ I like the simplicity of m0, m1 naming, but what if we involved qubit indexes 
   on it? E.g. m0, m1 is understood by time ordering, but they could happen the 
   same time and first was for qubit 0 and second for qubit 4. So a q0_m and    
  q4_m would be better. And if multiple measurements happened something like    
  q0_m0 q0_m1 etc. I don't know what is your take on this?                      

● Good instinct. Per-qubit naming makes refactors much more local — inserting a
  measurement upstream renames at most that qubit's later measurements, not
  every measurement after that point in the file. Let me think through the
  corner cases.

  The rule I'd propose

  schema-backed bus (BusRef with element + index):
  {element}{flat_index}_m{counter}
  raw-string bus (no metadata):                      m{counter}
  user-supplied name=:                               used verbatim

  Where:
  - flat_index is the bus's index flattened — 0 for q[0], 0_1 for c[0,1], etc.
  - counter is per (element, index) — measurements on q0 share one counter,
  measurements on q4 share another, raw-string measurements share a global one.
  - counter always starts at 0 and is always emitted — even when there's only
  one measurement on that qubit.

  So your example becomes:

  m_q0 = program.measure(q[0].readout, ...)   # q0_m0
  m_q4 = program.measure(q[4].readout, ...)   # q4_m0
  m_q0_b = program.measure(q[0].readout, ...) # q0_m1
  m_c = program.measure(c[0,1].readout, ...)  # c0_1_m0
  m_raw = program.measure("legacy_bus", ...)  # m0

  Why always include _m0, not bare q0_m

  I considered the "drop the counter when there's only one" form, and the
  renames-on-collision form. Both have a load-bearing problem:

  m = program.measure(q[0].readout, ...)     # bare form → name="q0_m"
  print(m.name)                              # "q0_m"

  # Later, second measurement on q0 arrives. The bare form has to retroactively
  # become "q0_m0" so the second can be "q0_m1" without colliding. But:

  program.measure(q[0].readout, ...)         # now first is renamed to "q0_m0"
  print(m.name)                              # still "q0_m" — handle is stale!

  The handle the user captured points to a name that no longer exists in the
  program. result.get(m) raises KeyError. Same issue if the handle was captured
  before saving and a second measurement was added afterward.

  Always-include-counter sidesteps it: the first measurement is already q0_m0,
  regardless of what comes later. Handles never go stale. The trade-off is one
  extra digit per name in the single-measurement-per-qubit case — small price
  for stable handles.

  Coupler edge case

  c[0,1] has index (0, 1). Flattened with _, you get c0_1_m0. The format
  technically has the same separator twice (_), so a reader could parse it two
  ways:

  - c0_1 + _m + 0 (correct)
  - c0 + _1_m + 0 (wrong)

  Humans handle this fine because they know couplers exist. Tooling that wants
  to parse names back to (element, index, counter) would need to be explicit.
  Acceptable trade-off — the name is for humans and stable identity, not machine
   parsing. If we later need it parseable, we add a
  MeasurementHandle.element/.index field that's populated at construction time
  (cheap to add).

  Counter scope details

  Two cases worth noting:

  Same (element, index), different bus kind. qblox.acquire(q[0].drive, ...) and
  program.measure(q[0].readout, ...) both target q0 — they share q0's counter.
  So if measure comes first, it's q0_m0; then acquire is q0_m1. Even though the
  buses differ, they're measuring the same qubit, and that's what _m refers to.
  (Including bus kind in the name — q0_readout_m0 — felt like noise that doesn't
   add disambiguation power.)

  Mixed schema and raw-string buses. A program that uses both gets q0_m0 next to
   m0. Visually distinct (the prefixes differ), no name collisions. Two separate
   counters silently in operation.

  Override is always available

  The user-supplied form sidesteps all of this:

  program.measure(q[0].readout, "r", "w", name="rabi_endpoint")

  So if q0_m0 feels noisy and you know there's only one measurement on q0, just
  pass name="q0_m" (or name="rabi_endpoint"). The auto-namer is a convenience,
  not a constraint.

  Concrete examples for the spec doc

  ┌─────────────────────────────┬────────────────┬─────────────┬───────────┐
  │             Bus             │     First      │ Second on   │ Third on  │
  │                             │  measurement   │    same     │   same    │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼────────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┤
  │ q[0].readout                │ q0_m0          │ q0_m1       │ q0_m2     │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼────────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┤
  │ q[4].readout                │ q4_m0          │ q4_m1       │ —         │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼────────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┤
  │ c[0,1].readout              │ c0_1_m0        │ c0_1_m1     │ —         │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼────────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┤
  │ qubit[7].readout (custom    │ qubit7_m0      │ qubit7_m1   │ —         │
  │ element name)               │                │             │           │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼────────────────┼─────────────┼───────────┤
  │ "legacy_bus" (raw string)   │ m0             │ m1          │ m2        │
  └─────────────────────────────┴────────────────┴─────────────┴───────────┘

  One thing to confirm

     include counter starting at 0 (recommended)

      154 +    # --- Measurement handles ---                             
      155 +                                                              
      156 +    def measurement_handles(self) -> list[MeasurementHandle]: 
      157 +        """Return a fresh :class:`MeasurementHandle` for every
          + measurement in the AST.                                      
      158 +                                                              
      159 +        Recovers handles in declaration order — the order :met
          +h:`measure`                                                   
      160 +        / vendor measurement ops were called. Useful after    
      161 +        :func:`qprogram.loads`, where the original Python hand
          +le locals are                                                 
      162 +        gone but the names survive in the AST.                
      163 +                                                              
      164 +        Returns a *new* list of fresh handle objects on every 
          +call; handles                                                 
      165 +        are structurally compared by name, so equality with pr
          +eviously-held                                                 
      166 +        handles (or those reconstructed by name) still works. 
      167 +        """                                                   
      168 +        return [MeasurementHandle(op.name) for op in _walk_mea
          +surement_ops(self._body)]                                     
      169 +                                                              
      170 +    def _allocate_measurement_name(self, bus: str, requested: 
          +str | None) -> str:                                           
      171 +        """Choose the name for a new measurement and verify un
          +iqueness.                                                     
      172 +                                                              
      173 +        - If ``requested`` is provided, it is used verbatim af
          +ter checking                                                  
      174 +          that no existing measurement already has that name. 
          +Raises                                                        
      175 +          :class:`ValueError` on collision.                   
      176 +        - Otherwise, an auto-name is generated using the conve
          +ntion:                                                        
      177 +                                                              
      178 +            - **Schema-backed bus** (``BusRef`` with ``element
          +`` and                                                        
      179 +              ``index``): ``{element}{flat_index}_m{counter}``
          +. ``flat_index``                                              
      180 +              flattens tuple indices with ``_`` (so ``c[0,1]``
          + →                                                            
      181 +              ``c0_1_m0``). ``counter`` is per-``(element, ind
          +ex)`` and                                                     
      182 +              always starts at ``0`` — so the second measureme
          +nt on q0 is                                                   
      183 +              ``q0_m1`` and the first measurement on q4 is ``q
          +4_m0``,                                                       
      184 +              regardless of source-order interleaving.        
      185 +                                                              
      186 +            - **Raw-string bus**: falls back to ``m{counter}``
          + with a                                                       
      187 +              global counter shared across all raw-string meas
          +urements.                                                     
      188 +                                                              
      189 +        The counters are not stored on the program — they are 
          +derived from                                                  
      190 +        the AST on each call. This keeps :func:`copy.deepcopy`
          +,                                                             
      191 +        ``with_waveforms``, and ``loads``/``dumps`` round-trip
          +s free of any                                                 
      192 +        hidden state, at the cost of one AST walk per measurem
          +ent                                                           
      193 +        construction. Programs with thousands of measurements 
          +would feel                                                    
      194 +        this; a memoised counter is a small future optimisatio
          +n.                                                            
      195 +        """                                                   
      196 +        used_names = {op.name for op in _walk_measurement_ops(
          +self._body)}                                                  
      197 +        if requested is not None:                             
      198 +            if not isinstance(requested, str) or not requested
          +:                                                             
      199 +                msg = f"measurement name must be a non-empty s
          +tring, got {requested!r}"                                     
      200 +                raise ValueError(msg)                         
      201 +            if requested in used_names:                       
      202 +                msg = (                                       
      203 +                    f"measurement name {requested!r} is alread
          +y used by another "                                           
      204 +                    f"measurement in this program"            
      205 +                )                                             
      206 +                raise ValueError(msg)                         
      207 +            return requested                                  
      208 +                                                              
      209 +        prefix = _measurement_name_prefix(bus)                
      210 +        # Per-prefix counter: walk used names, count how many 
          +already share                                                 
      211 +        # this prefix, return the first free index.           
      212 +        n = 0                                                 
      213 +        while f"{prefix}{n}" in used_names:                   
      214 +            n += 1                                            
      215 +        return f"{prefix}{n}"                                 
      216 +

      501 +def _walk_measurement_ops(block: Block) -> list[MeasurementOpe
          +ration]:                                                      
      502 +    """Yield every :class:`MeasurementOperation` in ``block`` 
          +in declaration order.                                         
      503 +                                                              
      504 +    Recursive depth-first walk. Used by :meth:`QProgram.measur
          +ement_handles`                                                
      505 +    and :meth:`QProgram._allocate_measurement_name` — handle e
          +numeration and                                                
      506 +    name-uniqueness checks both need to see every measurement 
          +currently in                                                  
      507 +    the AST, including those nested inside loops or other bloc
          +ks.                                                           
      508 +    """                                                       
      509 +    out: list[MeasurementOperation] = []                      
      510 +    for element in block.elements:                            
      511 +        if isinstance(element, Block):                        
      512 +            out.extend(_walk_measurement_ops(element))        
      513 +        elif isinstance(element, MeasurementOperation):       
      514 +            out.append(element)                               
      515 +    return out                                                
      516 +                                                              
      517 +                                                              
      518 +def _measurement_name_prefix(bus: str) -> str:                
      519 +    """Compute the auto-name prefix for a measurement on ``bus
          +``.

      521 +    Schema-backed buses (``BusRef`` with element + index metad
          +ata) get a                                                    
      522 +    per-qubit prefix — e.g. ``q0_m``, ``c0_1_m``. Raw-string b
          +uses fall                                                     
      523 +    back to a global ``m`` prefix. The caller appends a free i
          +nteger to                                                     
      524 +    produce the final name. See :meth:`QProgram._allocate_meas
          +urement_name`.

          +s not None:

      528 +            "_".join(str(i) for i in bus.index) if isinstance(
          +bus.index, tuple) else str(bus.index)                         
      529 +        )                                                     
      530 +        return f"{bus.element}{idx_str}_m"                    
      531 +    return "m"

  Naming convention (locked in):
  - Schema-backed bus: {element}{flat_index}_m{counter} — e.g. q0_m0, q4_m0,
  q0_m1, c0_1_m0.
  - Raw-string bus: m{counter} (global counter).
  - Counter is per-(element, index), always starts at 0, always emitted.
  - User-supplied name="..." accepts any non-empty string and skips the counter.
  - Counters are derived from the AST on each allocation (no hidden state
  survives across with_* / deepcopy / loads).
  - Core measure and vendor qblox.acquire share the same name pool (an acquire
  on q0 after a measure on q0 picks up q0_m1).

  Verified end-to-end:
  1. Auto-naming q0_m0 / q1_m0 / q0_m1 per qubit, with m0 / m1 fallback for raw
  strings.
  2. Custom name accepted; collision detected with a clear error; empty name
  rejected.
  3. Handle equality is structural by name (works in sets and as dict keys).
  4. Vendor acquire participates in the same counter as core measure.
  5. program.measurement_handles() returns handles in declaration order,
  including from nested blocks.
  6. result.get(handle), result.get("q0_m0"), result.get(0) all work; bus=
  filter applies before name/index lookup; out-of-range / unknown-name raise the
   right exceptions.
  7. .qp round-trip preserves names (and therefore handle identity).
  8. with_bus_mapping deepcopy preserves names.
  9. Auto-namer finds the first free index when names have been manually set out
   of order.
  10. Full regression: registry refactor + widened expressions + handles all
  coexist and round-trip stably.

  Lint & type:
  - ruff clean on both packages.
  - ty: 46 diagnostics; baseline before this task was 44. The +2 are two
  BusRef.element lookups in the new _measurement_name_prefix — same pattern as
  ~10 pre-existing identical sites in the codebase that the project already
  tolerates. The other potential delta (vendor.py generic constructor) is
  suppressed with paired # type: ignore[call-arg]  # ty:ignore[unknown-argument]
   because it's a documented design choice (no Protocol for
  MeasurementOperation's constructor arity).

  Out of scope, as flagged in the proposal:
  - ActiveReset handle return — still no name field; it's a complex
  orchestration whose measurement-ness deserves its own design pass.
  - classify(handle, ...) and program.if_(predicate) — depend on the handle and
  the widened Expression AST, both now in place. Either can land next without
  further AST work.
  - Per-shot vs averaged result shape — independent task.

  Foundations now in place for future tasks:
  - classify(handle) returns a Variable (or a typed equivalent);
  program.if_(eq(state, 1)) slots in with no AST changes.
  - Vendor authors can register any new measurement op by subclassing
  MeasurementOperation and using _append_measurement — no boilerplate, names
  auto-flow.
  - A future "measurement_handles by qubit" accessor is trivial — walk the AST,
  group by handle name prefix.

※ recap: Goal: evolve QProgram into a complete DSL with strong backwards-compat for future versions. We just finished §4 — `measure()` now returns a `MeasurementHandle` with per-qubit auto-naming. Next: pick the next brainstorm item to implement.

❯ Let's move to "Lock down Operation / Block introspection contract (variables, 
   buses, waveforms, attributes, visit) (§6)." What do you propose?             

● Before coding, here's what I'm thinking — including a couple of decisions that
   need your input.

  Why this is the next move

  Look at qprogram.py today and you see the cost of not having this contract:

  - _collect_buses(block) walks the AST hunting for attributes named "bus",
  "control_bus", "buses" — by string convention. Vendor authors must remember
  those names.
  - _walk_measurement_ops(block) does its own recursion.
  - _remap_buses(block) and _remap_waveforms(block) walk again, with the same
  string-name conventions hardcoded.
  - Each Operation subclass implements get_variables() differently — some are
  inconsistent or buggy. Measure doesn't have one at all (so it returns set()
  even though Measure references variables via waveform params).
  qblox.Acquire.get_variables checks isinstance(self.weights, Variable) but
  misses Expression inside a waveform.

  Every future analyzer/linter/diagram-tool would build a parallel set of walks
  unless we factor it out now. And every vendor Operation is silently obliged to
   implement get_variables correctly. Bake the contract in once.

  The contract (proposal)

  Five methods on both Operation and Block, plus two class-attribute conventions
   on Operation. All keyword-free, no abstract requirements — subclasses inherit
   working defaults.

  Class-level conventions on Operation

  Two tuples that name which __init__ parameters hold buses / waveforms:

  class Operation:
      BUS_ATTRS: ClassVar[tuple[str, ...]] = ("bus",)
      WAVEFORM_ATTRS: ClassVar[tuple[str, ...]] = ()

  The defaults match the common case. Subclasses override only when they differ:

  class Sync(Operation):
      BUS_ATTRS = ("buses",)                          # variadic list

  class ActiveReset(Operation):
      BUS_ATTRS = ("bus", "control_bus")              # two-bus op
      WAVEFORM_ATTRS = ("waveform", "weights", "reset_pulse")

  class Measure(MeasurementOperation):
      WAVEFORM_ATTRS = ("waveform", "weights")

  class Play(Operation):
      WAVEFORM_ATTRS = ("waveform",)

  class SetParameter(Operation):
      BUS_ATTRS = ()                                  # no bus; uses alias

  Five introspection methods

  class Operation:
      def variables(self) -> set[Variable]:
          """Every Variable referenced by this op, transitively (incl. inside 
  waveforms/expressions)."""

      def buses(self) -> set[str]:
          """Every bus name (raw string or BusRef) this op touches."""

      def waveforms(self) -> set[Waveform | IQWaveform | str]:
          """Every waveform (concrete or string alias) this op carries."""

      def attributes(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
          """This op's __init__ params as {name: value}. Mirrors the registry 
  serializer's view."""

      def walk(self) -> Iterator[Operation | Block]:
          """Yield self. (Operations are leaves.)"""
          yield self


  class Block:
      def variables(self) -> set[Variable]:
          """Union over child variables. Loop blocks also include their bound 
  variable."""

      def buses(self) -> set[str]:
          """Union over child buses."""

      def waveforms(self) -> set[Waveform | IQWaveform | str]:
          """Union over child waveforms."""

      def attributes(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
          """Block-specific data. Empty for plain Block; ForLoop/Loop/Average 
  expose their params."""

      def walk(self) -> Iterator[Block | Operation]:
          """Pre-order: self, then each child recursively."""
          yield self
          for el in self._elements:
              yield from el.walk()

  Default implementations cover every existing op

  def variables(self) -> set[Variable]:
      out: set[Variable] = set()
      for name, value in vars(self).items():
          if not name.startswith("_"):
              out |= _collect_variables(value)   # recursive: Variable / 
  Expression / Waveform / list
      return out

  def buses(self) -> set[str]:
      out: set[str] = set()
      for attr_name in self.BUS_ATTRS:
          value = getattr(self, attr_name, None)
          if isinstance(value, str):
              out.add(value)
          elif isinstance(value, list):
              out.update(v for v in value if isinstance(v, str))
      return out

  def waveforms(self) -> set[Waveform | IQWaveform | str]:
      return {getattr(self, n) for n in self.WAVEFORM_ATTRS if getattr(self, n,
  None) is not None}

  def attributes(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
      sig = inspect.signature(type(self).__init__)
      params = list(sig.parameters.values())[1:]   # skip self
      return {p.name: getattr(self, p.name) for p in params if hasattr(self,
  p.name)}

  The module-level _collect_variables(value) does the recursive descent: handles
   Variable, Expression (uses its .variables()), Waveform (walks its params),
  list/tuple (recurses), and ignores everything else.

  After this lands:
  - Measure.variables() correctly includes variables inside waveform/weights —
  fixes the existing latent bug.
  - qblox.Acquire.variables() correctly includes variables inside weights
  waveform parameters — fixes the existing partial bug.
  - Every new vendor op gets all four read-introspection methods for free; the
  only thing the author must do is set BUS_ATTRS/WAVEFORM_ATTRS when their names
   differ.

  What changes in qprogram.py

  The cluster of duck-typed walkers shrinks:

  # Before
  def _collect_buses(block):
      result = set()
      for el in block.elements:
          if isinstance(el, Block):
              result |= _collect_buses(el)
              continue
          for attr in ("bus", "control_bus"):
              value = getattr(el, attr, None)
              if isinstance(value, str):
                  result.add(value)
          buses_list = getattr(el, "buses", None)
          if isinstance(buses_list, list):
              result.update(b for b in buses_list if isinstance(b, str))
      return result

  @property
  def buses(self):
      return _collect_buses(self._body)

  becomes:

  @property
  def buses(self):
      return self._body.buses()

  _walk_measurement_ops becomes (op for op in block.walk() if isinstance(op, 
  MeasurementOperation)).

  _remap_buses / _remap_waveforms keep their mutating role but use
  BUS_ATTRS/WAVEFORM_ATTRS so vendors with unusual names just-work:

  def _remap_buses(block, mapping):
      for op in block.walk():
          if not isinstance(op, Operation):
              continue
          for attr in op.BUS_ATTRS:
              value = getattr(op, attr, None)
              if isinstance(value, str):
                  setattr(op, attr, mapping.get(value, value))
              elif isinstance(value, list):
                  setattr(op, attr, [mapping.get(v, v) for v in value])

  walk() for loop blocks

  Parallel has the only quirk — its loop headers (self.loops) live separately
  from self._elements. Its walk() yields the parallel itself, then walks each of
   its loop headers, then walks the body:

  class Parallel(Block):
      def walk(self):
          yield self
          for lp in self.loops:
              yield from lp.walk()
          for el in self._elements:
              yield from el.walk()

  Loop blocks override variables() and attributes():

  class ForLoop(Block):
      def variables(self):
          return super().variables() | {self.variable}

      def attributes(self):
          return {"variable": self.variable, "start": self.start, "stop":
  self.stop, "step": self.step}

  class Average(Block):
      def attributes(self):
          return {"shots": self.shots}

  What's removed

  - Operation.get_variables — replaced by variables(). We're pre-1.0 and the
  Skill prompt explicitly says don't add deprecation shims, so it's a rename,
  not a co-existence.
  - Per-class get_variables on every core operation — the default variables()
  does the right thing, so they're deleted.
  - The same on qblox.Acquire (and any other vendor op that defined one).
  - _collect_buses(block) helper in qprogram.py — replaced by block.buses().
  - _walk_measurement_ops — replaced by the registry-aware walk pattern.

  What's out of scope

  - Expression / Waveform introspection. They already have what they need
  (Expression.variables() exists; Waveform doesn't have an analyzer interface
  yet but isn't urgently needed). Same-shape introspection on them is a clean
  future task; doing it now would expand the diff without a concrete consumer.
  - Mutation visitor (map/replace). Read-only walk is enough for
  linters/analyzers. Tree rewriting is a separate design.
  - Free-variable analysis. variables() returns every variable referenced, not
  "free with respect to enclosing loop scopes". The free/bound distinction
  matters for compilation but isn't useful for the read-only analyzers we're
  enabling. Easy to add later.

  Three decisions worth confirming
● User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · Traversal API — generator or visitor callback? → walk() generator 
     (recommended)
     · Should BUS_ATTRS/WAVEFORM_ATTRS be class attributes, or computed from a 
     registry-side declaration? → Class attributes on the Operation subclass 
     (recommended)
     · Keep `get_variables()` as a deprecated alias for one cycle, or 
     hard-rename? → Hard rename to variables() (recommended)

● Confirmed. Setting up tasks.

● Write(qprogram/src/qprogram/operations/operation.py)
  ⎿  Added 165 lines, removed 6 lines
        1 +"""Operation base class and the introspection contract for AST
          + nodes.                                                       
        2 +                                                              
        3 +Every :class:`Operation` (and every :class:`~qprogram.blocks.B
          +lock`) exposes a                                              
        4 +small uniform read-only interface so analyzers, linters, compi
          +lers, diagram                                                 
        5 +tools — and the serializer itself — can walk the program witho
          +ut                                                            
        6 +``isinstance`` ladders or duck-typed attribute hunting.       
        7 +                                                              
        8 +The contract is five methods:                                 
        9 +                                                              
       10 +- :meth:`Operation.variables` — every :class:`Variable` refere
          +nced by this op,                                              
       11 +  transitively through expressions and waveform parameters.   
       12 +- :meth:`Operation.buses` — every bus name the op touches.    
       13 +- :meth:`Operation.waveforms` — every concrete waveform or str
          +ing alias the                                                 
       14 +  op carries.                                                 
       15 +- :meth:`Operation.attributes` — the op's data as a ``{name: v
          +alue}`` dict,                                                 
       16 +  mirroring the ``__init__`` signature.                       
       17 +- :meth:`Operation.walk` — a generator yielding the op itself;
          + ``Block`` 's                                                 
       18 +  override recurses through children.
