      158 +def _collect_variables(value: object) -> set[Variable]:       
      159 +    """Recursively gather every :class:`Variable` reachable fr
          +om ``value``.                                                 
      160 +                                                              
      161 +    Used by :meth:`Operation.variables` to walk arbitrary attr
          +ibute                                                         
      162 +    contents. Handles the four shapes that appear inside an op
          +eration:                                                      
      163 +                                                              
      164 +    - :class:`Variable` — direct reference, add it.           
      165 +    - :class:`Expression` — delegate to its ``variables()`` (w
          +hich

          +/MathFunc/…).

          +he

          +onstructor                                                    
      169 +      arguments come along.                                   
      170 +    - ``list`` / ``tuple`` — walk each element.               
      171 +

          +ntributes

          +the

          +rcing them

      184 +            if not name.startswith("_"):

      186 +        return out                                            
      187 +    if isinstance(value, (list, tuple)):                      
      188 +        out = set()                                           
      189 +        for item in value:                                    
      190 +            out |= _collect_variables(item)                   
      191 +        return out                                            
      192 +    return set()

       1 +"""Generic block container with the AST introspection contract.
       2 +                                                               
       3 +A :class:`Block` is a sequential container of operations and ne
         +sted blocks.                                                   
       4 +Beyond holding children, it implements the same five-method int
         +rospection                                                     
       5 +interface as :class:`~qprogram.operations.Operation` so the ent
         +ire AST can                                                    
       6 +be walked through a single uniform API. Loop subclasses (``ForL
         +oop``,                                                         
       7 +``Loop``, ``Average``, ``Parallel``) override the relevant piec
         +es to expose                                                   
       8 +their own data — loop variables, sweep parameters, shot counts.
       9 +"""

      15

      18 +    from qprogram.variable import Variable

     ...
      31  
      32      def append(self, element: Block | Operation) -> None:
      33          self._elements.append(element)
      34 +                                                               
      35 +    # -- introspection ----------------------------------------
         +--------------                                                 
      36 +                                                               
      37 +    def variables(self) -> set[Variable]:                      
      38 +        """Union of variables across all child elements.       
      39 +                                                               
      40 +        Loop subclasses override to also include the variable t
         +hey bind                                                       
      41 +        (the loop counter).                                    
      42 +        """                                                    
      43 +        out: set[Variable] = set()                             
      44 +        for el in self._elements:                              
      45 +            out |= el.variables()                              
      46 +        return out                                             
      47 +                                                               
      48 +    def buses(self) -> set[str]:                               
      49 +        """Union of bus references across all child elements.""
         +"                                                              
      50 +        out: set[str] = set()                                  
      51 +        for el in self._elements:                              
      52 +            out |= el.buses()                                  
      53 +        return out                                             
      54 +                                                               
      55 +    def waveforms(self) -> set[Waveform | IQWaveform | str]:   
      56 +        """Union of waveform references across all child elemen
         +ts."""                                                         
      57 +        out: set[Waveform | IQWaveform | str] = set()          
      58 +        for el in self._elements:                              
      59 +            out |= el.waveforms()                              
      60 +        return out                                             
      61 +                                                               
      62 +    def attributes(self) -> dict[str, Any]:                    
      63 +        """This block's own data attributes (empty for the plai
         +n container).                                                  
      64 +                                                               
      65 +        Loop subclasses override to expose their parameters: ``
         +ForLoop``                                                      
      66 +        exposes ``variable``/``start``/``stop``/``step``, ``Loo
         +p`` exposes                                                    
      67 +        ``variable``/``values``, ``Average`` exposes ``shots``.
         + The default                                                   
      68 +        is empty because a plain :class:`Block` carries no data
         + of its own                                                    
      69 +        beyond its children.                                   
      70 +        """                                                    
      71 +        return {}                                              
      72 +                                                               
      73 +    def walk(self) -> Iterator[Block | Operation]:             
      74 +        """Pre-order traversal: yield this block, then each chi
         +ld recursively.                                                
      75 +                                                               
      76 +        Pairs with :meth:`Operation.walk` (which yields just th
         +e op).                                                         
      77 +        Together they let callers do ``for node in program.body
         +.walk()`` to                                                   
      78 +        visit every node in declaration order without writing r
         +ecursion.                                                      
      79 +        """                                                    
      80 +        yield self                                             
      81 +        for el in self._elements:                              
      82 +            yield from el.walk()

      16 +    """Run multiple loops concurrently. Created via the | opera
         +tor on loop contexts.                                          
      17  
      18 +    A :class:`Parallel` has a small structural quirk: its **loo
         +p headers**                                                    
      19 +    (the ``ForLoop`` / ``Loop`` instances composed via ``|``) l
         +ive in                                                         
      20 +    ``self.loops`` rather than in ``self._elements``. The body 
         +operations                                                     
      21 +    that run under all those loops jointly live in ``_elements`
         +` as usual.                                                    
      22 +    The introspection overrides below thread the loops back int
         +o the union                                                    
      23 +    so analyzers see the bound loop variables and the headers t
         +hemselves                                                      
      24 +    when walking.                                              
      25 +    """                                                        
      26 +                                                               
      27      def __init__(self, loops: list[ForLoop | Loop]) -> None:
      28          super().__init__()

      30 +                                                               
      31 +    def variables(self) -> set[Variable]:                      
      32 +        """Include each composed loop's bound variable plus chi
         +ld variables."""                                               
      33 +        out = super().variables()                              
      34 +        for lp in self.loops:                                  
      35 +            out |= lp.variables()                              
      36 +        return out                                             
      37 +                                                               
      38 +    def attributes(self) -> dict[str, Any]:                    
      39 +        """Expose the composed loop headers."""                
      40 +        return {"loops": list(self.loops)}                     
      41 +                                                               
      42 +    def walk(self) -> Iterator[Block | Operation]:             
      43 +        """Yield self, each composed loop header (as a leaf), t
         +hen body children."""                                          
      44 +        yield self                                             
      45 +        for lp in self.loops:                                  
      46 +            yield from lp.walk()                               
      47 +        for el in self._elements:                              
      48 +            yield from el.walk()

● Done. Summary of the introspection-contract task:

  The contract, now uniform on every AST node:

  ┌──────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────┐
  │    Method    │            Operation             │         Block         │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │              │ Recursive walk over public       │ Union over children;  │
  │ variables()  │ attrs, picking up Variables in   │ loop blocks add their │
  │              │ Expressions, Waveforms, lists    │  bound variable       │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ buses()      │ Walks BUS_ATTRS-named attrs;     │ Union over children   │
  │              │ handles scalars + lists          │                       │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ waveforms()  │ Walks WAVEFORM_ATTRS-named attrs │ Union over children   │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │              │ {param_name: value} derived from │ Block-specific data   │
  │ attributes() │  __init__ signature              │ (empty default; loops │
  │              │                                  │  expose params)       │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ walk()       │ Yields self                      │ Pre-order: self +     │
  │              │                                  │ recurse               │
  └──────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────┘

  Two class-attribute conventions on Operation:

  class Operation:
      BUS_ATTRS: ClassVar[tuple[str, ...]] = ("bus",)
      WAVEFORM_ATTRS: ClassVar[tuple[str, ...]] = ()

  Vendor authors and new core ops opt into the right shape by setting these next
   to __init__. The defaults match the common case so most ops set nothing.

✻ Crunched for 16m 4s

❯ Is the `attributes` method really useful? It seems boilerplate to me, and     
  repeats information: the properties are already there. Why did we add it?     

● You're right. Let me think through this honestly.

  What attributes() was supposed to do

  The original brainstorm proposed it as part of the contract because the idea
  was that the registry-driven serializer would call op.attributes() to get the
  data dict and then format it. If that wiring had happened, attributes() would
  be load-bearing for serialization.

  What it actually does

  Look at how it's currently used:

  ┌────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┐
  │               Caller               │               Status                │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ _specs.default_serialize_operation │ Uses inspect.signature directly,    │
  │                                    │ not attributes()                    │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ _specs.default_parse_operation     │ Same — direct signature inspection  │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Anything in qprogram.py            │ Nothing                             │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Anything in qprogram-qblox/        │ Nothing                             │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ The new smoke tests                │ Only thing that calls it — and only │
  │                                    │  to test it                         │
  └────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┘

  So we shipped a method that has zero non-test consumers, on a public-ish
  surface (it's not underscore-prefixed). The implementation is one call to
  inspect.signature + a dict comprehension — exactly what any caller would write
   themselves.

  Your point holds up

  - The data already exists on the instance: play.bus, play.waveform,
  average.shots. Direct attribute access is the obvious form.
  - A generic consumer that wants "everything this node carries" gets it from
  vars(node) (the instance __dict__) for free, no inspect import, no method
  dispatch.
  - The override-per-Block-subclass (ForLoop.attributes, Loop.attributes,
  Average.attributes, Parallel.attributes) is boilerplate that restates instance
   attributes Python already gives me.
  - It violates the Skill instruction I'm supposed to follow: "Don't add 
  features, refactor, or introduce abstractions beyond what the task requires."
  attributes() is a speculative abstraction — it serves a future consumer that
  doesn't exist.

  The other four contract methods (variables, buses, waveforms, walk) do earn
  their place: each transforms the raw data into something a consumer would
  otherwise have to compute themselves (recursive walk through waveforms; union
  across children; type-driven filtering). attributes() doesn't transform
  anything — it just re-packages.

  Recommendation: remove it

  Delete attributes() from Operation and Block, and the four overrides on
  ForLoop/Loop/Average/Parallel. The contract becomes a tighter four-method
  surface:

  variables() — answers "what variables does this touch?"
  buses()     — answers "what buses does this touch?"
  waveforms() — answers "what waveforms does this carry?"
  walk()      — answers "how do I traverse the subtree?"

  If a future tool genuinely needs the "param-name-to-value" view, it can
  either:
  - Do vars(node) (often enough).
  - Do inspect.signature(type(node).__init__) itself (the same one-liner I wrote
   in attributes()).
  - Or we add it back, with a real consumer to justify it.

  Want me to delete it now?

  Files touched:

  File: operations/operation.py

    attributes() is not in the contract (pointing to vars(op) /
  inspect.signature

  ────────────────────────────────────────

  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: blocks/loop.py
  Change: Removed attributes() override. variables() override stays.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: blocks/average.py
  Change: Removed attributes() override (the only override on the class — now
    just a constructor).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: blocks/parallel.py
  Change: Removed attributes() override; dropped Any import. variables() and
    walk() overrides stay.

  The contract is now exactly four methods:

  variables()  — every Variable referenced (recursively through expressions,
  waveforms, lists)
  buses()      — every bus name touched (driven by BUS_ATTRS)
  waveforms()  — every waveform / alias carried (driven by WAVEFORM_ATTRS)
  walk()       — pre-order generator over the subtree

  Lint & type:
  - ruff clean on both packages.
  - ty: 40 diagnostics, down from 46 baseline (and 46 going into the §6 task).
  All are pre-existing patterns (BusRef __slots__ introspection, PEP 695 type
  syntax, expression-arg variance from §3) — none introduced by §6 or this
  cleanup.

  All 11 §6 smoke tests still pass. The loop-block "metadata" that attributes()
  used to package is still accessible — through the obvious direct attribute
  paths it was repackaging:

  average.shots               # was: average.attributes()["shots"]
  for_loop.variable, .start, .stop, .step    # was: for_loop.attributes()[...]
  loop.values                 # was: loop.attributes()["values"]
  parallel.loops              # was: parallel.attributes()["loops"]

  A consumer that genuinely wants the param-name-to-value dict can still call
  vars(node) or inspect.signature(type(node).__init__) themselves — and now we
  wait for an actual consumer to materialize before re-adding the helper,
  instead of shipping a speculative one.

※ recap: We're locking down QProgram's pre-1.0 foundations so future versions evolve cleanly; the §6 Operation/Block introspection contract just landed (variables, buses, waveforms, walk), with `attributes()` deleted as speculative. Next: pick the next foundation task to tackle.

❯ Can you summarize again the rest of the proposals that are not implemented    
  yet? Be sure not to forget anything and start the ones who are most           
  important.                                                                    

● Here's the full state of the original "lock-it-down-before-1.0" list, ordered
  from most important to least. The first five would be my recommendation for
  what to take on next; everything below is either small, additive, or
  speculative.

  What's done

  - §1 Registry-driven parser & writer — all dispatch in .qp goes through
  registries (ops, blocks, sweep generators)
  - §3 Widened Expression AST — comparisons, logical, math, where, with the
  __bool__ guard
  - §4 MeasurementHandle from measure() — per-qubit auto-naming, handles into
  result lookup
  - §6 Operation/Block introspection contract — variables(), buses(),
  waveforms(), walk(), plus BUS_ATTRS/WAVEFORM_ATTRS conventions
  - §2 Attribute-bag pattern — partial: operation optional params already emit
  as key=value. The pattern hasn't been pushed onto block headers (average 1000:
   is still positional, no average shots=1000:) because no block has grown
  optional params yet. Not urgent.
  - §13.6 parallel() legacy mention — already removed

  ---
  Still pending — ordered by importance

  Tier 1 — finish the lock-it-down list (cheap now, expensive later)

  1. §9 — Always inline-serialize schemas

  Today schema: transmon is a one-line keyword reference into a hardcoded preset
   class. If BusSchema.transmon() ever gains or renames a bus, every existing
  .qp file silently inherits the change. One small writer change — always emit
  the expanded element/bus form, even for built-in presets — locks the file
  format off from preset drift forever. The preset keyword is a Python
  constructor convenience; the file format doesn't need to know about it.

  Cost: ~30 lines moved in the writer, a few lines deleted in the parser, the
  preset-keyword grammar branch goes away.

  2. §13.1 — Replace save_adc: bool with return: list[str]

  measure(...) currently has save_adc=False — a boolean that has nowhere to grow
   when classification arrives ("state" projection), or post-selection masks, or
   alternative readout projections. Replace with returns="iq" (default) or
  returns="iq,state" or returns="iq,raw" etc.

  Doing this before classification lands means classification can flow into the
  existing field rather than spawning a parallel API. Same idea for
  qblox.acquire.

  Cost: small. Touches the Measure and Acquire classes, their docs, and the .qp
  examples.

  3. §11 — Hashability + structural equality on AST nodes

  Expression nodes already are (it was a §3 deliverable). Operations and Blocks
  aren't. Make them so, and the following become free:
  - diff tooling (qp diff a.qp b.qp becomes a set difference)
  - caching compiled programs (the hash is the cache key)
  - programmatic rewrites (if op == pattern: replace_with(...))
  - structural deduplication of identical waveforms in serialized output

  The discipline is: every constructor parameter immutable, define __hash__ /
  __eq__ based on vars(self). Vendor authors get it for free if they inherit
  from Operation.

  Cost: One method on Operation and Block plus a docstring stating the contract.
   Subclasses need no changes unless they want to deviate.

  4. §13.7 — Error taxonomy

  Define the base exception hierarchy now even if implementations are minimal:

  QProgramError
  ├── ValidationError       (schema/bus/waveform mismatch at construction)
  ├── UnsupportedOperationError  (platform-side: op not implementable on this
  hardware)
  ├── BusNotAvailableError       (platform-side: bus not exposed)
  ├── WaveformResolutionError    (alias not bound by with_waveforms())
  ├── CompilationError           (platform-side: compile failed)
  └── HardwareError              (platform-side: runtime failure)

  Concrete platforms then raise the right base, and users get to write except 
  WaveformResolutionError once across every backend. This was the open question
  at the end of the DSL spec — answering it now means the answer is part of the
  1.0 contract.

  Cost: Tiny. One new module of class definitions; refactor the small number of
  existing TypeError/ValueError raises in the core to subclass ValidationError.

  5. §8 — Reserve future keywords

  The lexer and Variable.id validator should already reject as identifiers the
  keywords future versions are likely to need:

  if, else, elif, while, until, break, continue, return, fragment, def, gate,
  case, match, repeat, barrier, align, align_left, align_right, parallel, where,
   let, const, import, from, as, true, false, null.

  Also reserve dotted prefixes for vendor namespaces only (no core.foo allowed
  as an op name).

  Cost: One frozenset constant in the parser plus a check in Variable.__init__.
  Same principle as _ID_RE today — just stricter.

  ---
  Tier 2 — punch-list items from §13

  Smaller refactors. Each one is half a day; doing them now is much cheaper than
   after 1.0.

  6. §13.4 — Split average(shots) into repeat(N) + an average flag

  average(shots=N) bundles a count with a meaning (collapse the dimension by
  mean). Split into:
  - repeat(N) block — run children N times, keep all N shots
  - measure(..., average=True) (or block-level average=True attribute) —
  collapse on read

  Lets a future single-shot RB program work without inventing a parallel
  "single_shot" block, and lets per-shot post-selection slot in cleanly.

  7. §13.2 — Generalize set_offset

  set_offset(bus, offset_path0, offset_path1=None) leaks Qblox's I/Q path
  vocabulary into the core API. Recast as set_offset(bus, i=..., q=...) or
  set_offset(bus, *values) and let vendors specialize via attributes.

  8. §13.3 — set_parameter channel_id

  Move from a fixed kwarg into the trailing attribute bag (channel_id=N becomes
  one of any number of vendor-specific channel selectors). Pure consistency with
   the attribute-bag pattern.

  9. §13.5 — CrosstalkMatrix decision

  The DSL spec marks this section with (?). Either commit to it as a first-class
   core construct (with proper .qp serialization rather than today's
  set_crosstalk crosstalk stub) or move it to a vendor extension. Don't ship 1.0
   with a maybe.

  ---
  Tier 3 — nice-to-haves that can wait

  10. §5 — Unify ForLoop/Loop into a single Sweep block

  Cosmetic AST cleanup. Both classes work fine today; the sweep-generator
  registry is what made them extensible. Merging is reasonable but doesn't
  unblock anything.

  11. §7 — Block IDs for tooling

  Auto-assign stable integer/UUID IDs to each Block at construction time,
  exposed via block.id. Lets future tools address blocks by stable path. Trivial
   to add when a consumer asks for it.

  12. §12 — Rest of the QProgramResult API

  The handle-based lookup landed in §4. Other shape decisions worth making
  (eventually):
  - result.to_dataset() -> xr.Dataset for "everything in one xarray container"
  - result.metadata() -> dict for runtime info (timestamps, platform
  identifiers, etc.)
  - result.measurements() already exists as a property

  Wait for a real consumer to drive the exact shape.

  13. §10 — Formal feature flag system

  #!QProgram 1.0 plus features: classification mid_circuit_feedback declarations
   in the header. Useful if we plan to ship experimental constructs behind
  opt-ins; less critical otherwise. Pure-version-bump evolution gets us most of
  the way.

  ---
  Feature track (post-1.0 lock-down)

  These come after the lock-it-down work, and the foundations are mostly in
  place thanks to §3, §4, §6:

  - Mid-circuit feedback: classify(handle), program.if_(predicate), repeat,
  while, break, continue. §3 (boolean expressions) and §4 (handles) are the AST
  scaffolding.
  - Alignment: barrier, align_left, align_right. Additive control-flow blocks.
  - Per-shot results / single-shot mode: enabled by the §13.4 split.
  - Composability: @qp.fragment parameterised reusable blocks; .qp-side fragment
   foo(bus): syntax.
  - Calibration object: travel calibration data alongside the program rather
  than as ambient state.
  - Waveform libraries: WaveformLibrary first-class, calibration-tooling writes
  .qpl files.
  - Tooling: qp fmt, qp lint, qp analyze, qp draw, qp diff, LSP. All trivial to
  build now that the introspection contract from §6 is in place.
  - More waveforms: Tukey, Blackman, Modulated, Chirp, GaussianFlatTop, etc.
  - Bus connectivity graph + per-bus capability annotations on schemas.
  - dtype on Variable (optional metadata for int/float/bool).

  ---
  My recommendation for what's next

  Take §9 (inline schemas) and §13.1 (save_adc → returns) together as a single
  small task — they're both writer/spec changes that touch nothing else. Then
  §11 (AST hashability) as the foundation for any future tooling. Then §13.7 
  (error taxonomy) plus §8 (reserved keywords) — both small and
  lock-down-worthy.

  After that, the lock-it-down list is essentially done and we can move into the
   feature track. The biggest single feature lying right there is conditional 
  control flow (classify + if_), since §3 and §4 have already done all the
  upstream work.

      144 +def normalize_returns(value: str | Iterable[str]) -> tuple[str
          +, ...]:                                                       
      145 +    """Coerce a ``returns`` argument into the canonical ``tupl
          +e[str, ...]``.                                                
      146 +                                                              
      147 +    Accepts the three input shapes users naturally reach for: 
      148 +                                                              
      149 +    - a comma-separated string — ``"iq,raw"`` — tokenised on c
          +ommas.                                                        
      150 +    - any iterable of strings — ``["iq", "raw"]`` / ``("iq",)`
          +`.                                                            
      151 +    - a single string token — ``"iq"`` (degenerates to a one-e
          +lement tuple).                                                
      152 +                                                              
      153 +    Empty entries (from doubled commas or whitespace tokens) a
          +re dropped;                                                   
      154 +    a fully-empty input raises :class:`ValueError` so the fiel
          +d never ends                                                  
      155 +    up as an empty tuple silently. The canonical tuple form is
          + used for                                                     
      156 +    storage, equality, and the ``.qp`` serializer's comma-join
          +ed output                                                     
      157 +    (see the writer's ``serialize_value``).                   
      158 +                                                              
      159 +    No restriction on string contents at this layer — platform
          +s decide                                                      
      160 +    which return-type strings they recognise (``"iq"``, ``"raw
          +"``,                                                          
      161 +    ``"state"``, …) and raise their own error for unsupported 
          +ones.                                                         
      162 +    """                                                       
      163 +    if isinstance(value, str):                                
      164 +        parts = [p.strip() for p in value.split(",")]         
      165 +    else:                                                     
      166 +        parts = [str(p).strip() for p in value]

● Update(.specs/qp-file-format.md)
  ⎿  Added 13 lines, removed 22 lines
      104  
      105  # 3a. Schema Declaration
      106  
      107 -Buses fall into three categories, each with its own serializat
          -ion:                                                          
      107 +Buses fall into two categories on the wire:                   
      108  
      109  1. **Plain string** — `play "drive_q0_bus" pulse`. No schema s
           ection. The bus is just a string, no metadata.
      110 -2. **Preset schema** (one of the built-in `BusSchema` subclass
          -es — `transmon`, `transmon_coupled`, `flux_tunable_transmon`, 
          -`flux_tunable_transmon_coupled`, `fluxonium`, `fluxonium_coupl
          -ed`). One-liner declaration; the parser instantiates the prese
          -t class.                                                      
      111 -3. **Custom / dynamic schema** (`BusSchema()` populated via `a
          -dd_element`, or a user subclass of `BusSchema`). Full inline d
          -eclaration so the parser can rebuild the structure without the
          - user's Python class.                                         
      110 +2. **Schema-backed** — declared in a single inline `schema:` b
          +lock at the top of the file. Operations then reference buses b
          +y path: `play q[0].drive pulse`.                              
      111  
      112 +The Python side offers preset constructors (`BusSchema.transmo
          +n()`, `.fluxonium()`, …) and a dynamic builder (`BusSchema()` 
          ++ `add_element(...)`) as construction-time conveniences, but *
          +*both serialize to the same inline form** — the file format re
          +cords the structural element/bus contents, not which construct
          +or the user reached for. This keeps `.qp` files immune to sile
          +nt drift if a preset class ever grows a new bus or renames a k
          +ind.                                                          
      113 +                                                              
      114  **One schema per program.** A `.qp` file declares at most one 
           `schema:` block (written before `body:` and after `require` / 
           `metadata:`). The single-schema model lets operation bus refer
           ences be terse paths — `q[0].drive` rather than `transmon.q[0]
           .drive` — because there's only one schema they could resolve a
           gainst. Programs that need multiple chip layouts can still use
            plain-string buses for the second-citizen ones, or roll them 
           into a single inline schema with disjoint element names.
      115  
      115 -## 3a.1 Preset (one-liner) form                               
      116 +## 3a.1 Inline schema form                                    
      117  
      118  ```
      118 -schema: transmon                                    # default 
          -naming                                                        
      119 -schema: transmon("{kind}_{element}{index}_bus")     # custom n
          -aming pattern                                                 
      120 -```                                                           
      121 -                                                              
      122 -- `<kind>` must be a built-in preset (else the parser errors a
          -nd points at the inline form).                                
      123 -- Optional `("<pattern>")` after the kind sets the `BusNaming`
          - pattern. The pattern uses `{element}`, `{index}`, `{kind}` pl
          -aceholders.                                                   
      124 -                                                              
      125 -## 3a.2 Custom / dynamic (inline) form                        
      126 -                                                              
      127 -```                                                           
      119  schema:
      120    naming: "{element}{index}/{kind}"                # optional;
            default if omitted
      121    element q:
     ...
      126      flux    info=single
      127  ```
      128  
      138 -- `schema:` with no kind on the same line opens an inline body
          -.                                                             
      139 -- Optional `naming: "<pattern>"` line; the default is `"{eleme
          -nt}{index}/{kind}"`.                                          
      129 +- The header is exactly `schema:` (no inline content on the sa
          +me line).                                                     
      130 +- Optional `naming: "<pattern>"` line; the default is `"{eleme
          +nt}{index}/{kind}"`. The pattern supports the placeholders `{e
          +lement}`, `{index}`, `{kind}`.                                
      131  - One or more `element <elem>:` blocks. Each lists bus kinds a
           s `<kind> info=<channel>[+acquires]` lines.
      132  - `info=` channel is required (`single` or `IQ`); the only cur
           rently defined flag is `acquires` (bus has an ADC). Examples: 
           `info=single`, `info=IQ`, `info=IQ+acquires`.
      133  - Indentation: 2 spaces for `naming:` / `element` headers, 4 s
           paces for bus-kind lines.
      134  
      144 -User-typed `BusSchema` subclasses (e.g. `MyChipSchema` with `K
          -IND = "my_chip"` and `@property` factories) serialize via the 
          -inline form — the Python class doesn't survive round-trip, but
          - the element/bus structure does, so the loaded program is stru
          -cturally complete.                                            
      135 +User-typed `BusSchema` subclasses (e.g. `MyChipSchema` with `@
          +property` factories) serialize through the same form — the Pyt
          +hon class identity doesn't survive round-trip, but the element

      140  
     ...
      150  - Plain string buses stay quoted (`"raw_bus"`), as in case 1.
      151  - The parser resolves each path against `program.schema` (the 
           one and only schema attached to the program) to produce a `Bus
           Ref` with full metadata (`kind`, `element`, `index`, `channel`
           , `acquires`). Validation (`_validate_waveform_channel`, `_val
           idate_acquires`) works post-load on these refs.
      152  
      162 -## 3a.4 Round-trip notes                                      
      153 +## 3a.3 Round-trip notes                                      
      154  
      164 -- Built-in preset → one-liner. Loader instantiates the same pr
          -eset class and attaches it as `program.schema`.               
      165 -- Custom/dynamic schema → inline. Loader rebuilds a `BusSchema
          -` via `add_element()` calls and attaches it as `program.schema
          -`. The schema instance is dynamic, not the original user subcl
          -ass.                                                          
      155 +- Whether the writer is given a preset (`BusSchema.transmon()`
          +) or a dynamic builder (`BusSchema().add_element(...)`), the s
          +ame inline form is emitted.                                   
      156 +- On load, the parser rebuilds a dynamic `BusSchema` via `add_
          +element()` calls and attaches it as `program.schema`. The sche
          +ma instance is dynamic, not the original Python preset/subclas
          +s.                                                            
      157  - Plain string buses → no declaration; remain plain strings on
            reload.
      158  - Mixed: a single program can mix schema-backed bus paths with
            plain-string buses freely.
      159  
      160  The parser rejects:
      161  
      162  - a second `schema:` declaration in the same file,
      172 -- unknown preset `<kind>` with no inline body,                
      163 +- a `schema:` line with trailing content (the inline form is t
          +he only form),

● Update(.specs/qprogram-dsl.md)

      147  
      148  ### Serialization round-trip
      149  
      150 -`BusRef` metadata is preserved across `.qp` round-trip via a s
          -ingle optional `schema:` declaration at the top of the file. T
          -he format follows the **three cases** a user actually creates 
          -buses through:                                                
      150 +`BusRef` metadata is preserved across `.qp` round-trip via a s
          +ingle optional `schema:` declaration at the top of the file. T
          +he format has **two on-the-wire cases**:                      
      151  
      152  1. **Plain string** — written as `play "drive_q0_bus" pulse`. 
           No schema declaration, no metadata.
      153 -2. **Built-in preset** (e.g. `BusSchema.transmon()`, passed as
          - `QProgram(schema=...)`) — one-liner declaration: `schema: tra
          -nsmon` (or `schema: transmon("<pattern>")` with custom naming)
          -. Operations reference buses as paths: `play q[0].drive pulse`
          -. On load, the parser reinstantiates the preset class, attache
          -s it as `program.schema`, and resolves each path through it.  
      154 -3. **Custom / dynamic schema** (`BusSchema()` populated via `a
          -dd_element`, or a user subclass of `BusSchema` with `KIND` set
          -) — `schema:` followed by an inline `element`/bus body; the pa
          -rser rebuilds a `BusSchema` via `add_element()` so the structu
          -ral data round-trips even though the user's Python class doesn
          -'t.                                                           
      153 +2. **Schema-backed** — declared via a single inline `schema:` 
          +block at the top of the file. Operations then reference buses 
          +as paths: `play q[0].drive pulse`. On load, the parser rebuild
          +s the schema by walking the `element`/bus declarations.       
      154  
      155 +The Python side ships preset constructors (`BusSchema.transmon
          +()`, `BusSchema.fluxonium()`, …) and a dynamic builder (`BusSc
          +hema()` + `add_element(...)`) as construction-time convenience
          +s. Both serialize to the **same inline form** — the file forma
          +t records the structural element/bus contents, not which const
          +ructor the user reached for. This keeps `.qp` files immune to 
          +silent drift if a preset class ever grows a new bus or renames
          + a kind.                                                      
      156 +                                                              
      157  A program holds **at most one** schema (set via the `QProgram(
           schema=...)` constructor). That's why the operation bus paths 
           drop the schema-name prefix — there's only one schema to resol
           ve against. Plain-string buses can still coexist with a schema
           -backed program: anything quoted in the body sidesteps the sch
           ema entirely.
      158  
      158 -User-typed subclasses (`MyChipSchema` etc.) should set `KIND =
          - "my_chip"` as a class attribute. The writer serializes them v
          -ia the inline form (case 3), preserving the structure; on load
          - the user gets a dynamic `BusSchema` with the same elements, j
          -ust without the original Python class identity.               
      159 +User-typed subclasses (`MyChipSchema` etc.) serialize through 
          +the same inline form, preserving the element/bus structure. On
          + load the user gets a dynamic `BusSchema` with the same elemen
          +ts, just without the original Python class identity.          
      160

      162  When a bus is referenced through the schema, operations valida
           te at program-construction time:

❯ Should the strings in the `returns` iterable be specific literals? I can only 
   think the `iq`, `raw` and `state` variants.                                  

● Good question. Let me lay out the real tradeoffs.

  What's at stake

  The three you listed (iq, raw, state) are the canonical core set today. The
  question is whether to bake them into the type system, and how rigid that
  should be.

  The argument for constraining them:

  - IDE autocomplete: returns="i" → suggests "iq".
  - Catches typos: returns="stae" errors at type-check time instead of silently
  producing a "no such projection" platform error.
  - Self-documenting signature.

  The argument against:

  - The brainstorm direction has consistently been "platforms decide what they
  recognise". Locking the type at the core blocks vendor extensions from
  accepting their own tokens — e.g., qblox.acquire(..., 
  returns="iq,qblox_pre_demod").
  - Future-likely additions even within core: a "magnitude"/"phase" projection
  split (vs IQ), classification variants ("state_0_1" vs "state_0_1_2"),
  post-selection flags, shot-index labels. Each new token in a strict-Literal
  world is a major bump.
  - The .qp format already serializes any string — so the type and the file
  format would have different validity rules.

  Four ways to spell this

  Option: A. Current (free-form)
  API signature: returns: str | Iterable[str]
  What it gets you: Vendor-extensible, simplest
  What it costs: No autocomplete, no typo checking
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: B. Literal + str escape
  API signature: returns: Literal["iq","raw","state"] | 
    Iterable[Literal["iq","raw","state"]] | str | Iterable[str]
  What it gets you: Best-effort autocomplete on the canonical three, but str
    widens it for vendor use
  What it costs: Verbose signature; the | str widens the Literal back, so static

    checkers may or may not still suggest the three
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: C. Strict Literal
  API signature: returns: Literal["iq","raw","state"] | 
    Iterable[Literal["iq","raw","state"]]
  What it gets you: Strongest type safety, autocomplete works cleanly
  What it costs: Blocks future tokens (every addition is a code change). Vendor
    extensions need cast() or # type: ignore to accept custom tokens
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: D. Free-form + named constants
  API signature: returns: str | Iterable[str] + qp.Returns.IQ, qp.Returns.RAW,
    qp.Returns.STATE
  What it gets you: Free-form storage; users opting into the canonical names get

    autocomplete via the constant access
  What it costs: Two ways to spell each name ("iq" and qp.Returns.IQ); slightly
    verbose

  My recommendation: D (free-form + named constants)

  It's the cleanest non-restrictive option. Concretely:

  class Returns:
      """Canonical return-type tokens for ``measure()`` and vendor measurement 
  ops.

      The token set is open — platforms decide which ones they recognise, and
      vendor extensions can introduce additional tokens (e.g. ``"qblox_raw"``).
      The constants below are the core-defined canonical tokens; using them
      instead of bare strings gets you IDE autocomplete and protects against
      typos.
      """
      IQ = "iq"
      RAW = "raw"
      STATE = "state"  # forward-looking — populated when classification lands

  Users who want type safety write returns=(qp.Returns.IQ, qp.Returns.RAW);
  users who don't write returns=("iq", "raw"). Same data on the wire. Vendor
  extensions can add their own constants in the same namespace pattern.

  The cost is one extra small module / class and a one-line note in the spec
  listing the canonical set. The benefit is that we don't paint ourselves into a
   corner — every Literal commitment we make now is something a future feature
  has to dance around.

  The Skill prompt I'm running under explicitly tells me to avoid adding
  abstractions beyond what the task requires, so I want to ask before committing
   to even this much. Which would you like?

● User declined to answer questions

      constants (recommended) / Strict Literal["iq", "raw", "state"] / Keep 
     current free-form, just document the canonical set)
